&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mu\Big(\bigcap_{j=-1}^{l}\big(T^{-p_{1}(n+j)}U_{j}^{(1)}\cap\cdots\cap T^{-p_{d}(n+j)}U_{j}^{(d)}\big)\Big)\\
&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mu\Big(\bigcap_{j=-1}^{l}\bigcap_{k=1}^{d}T^{-(p_{k}(n+j)-p_{k}(j))-p_{k}(j)}U_{j}^{(k)}\big)\\
&\geq\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mu\Big(\bigcap_{j=-1}^{l}\big(T^{-(p_{1}(n+j)-p_{1}(j))}V\cap\cdots\cap T^{-(p_{d}(n+j)-p_{d}(j))}V\big)\Big).\end{split}

Since
{\rm supp}\ \mu=X
, we have
\mu(V)>0
.
By Theorem
2.25
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\mu\Big(\bigcap_{j=-1}^{l}\big(T^{-(p_{1}(n+j)-p_{1}(j))}V\cap\cdots\cap T^{-(p_{d}(n+j)-p_{d}(j))}V\big)\Big)>0,

which implies that
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(U)>0
. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

We have

(1)

By the proof of Theorem
3.7
, we actually proved the following equality:

{\rm supp}\ \mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\overline{\{\sigma^{n}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}:x\in{\rm supp}\mu,\ n\in\mathbb{Z}\}}.

When
(X,T)
is minimal, we have
{\rm supp}\ \mu=X
for each
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{T}(X)
and
{\rm supp}\ \mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})
.
Similar statement holds for
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.

(2)

By Theorem
3.7
, if
(X,T)
is minimal, then
(N_{\infty}({X},\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is an
E
-system, i.e. it is transitive and have an invariant measure with a full support. In fact we can say more, i.e. it was proved
[
26
]
that

(N_{\infty}({X},\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is an
M
-system, i.e., it is transitive and the set of
\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle
-minimal points is dense.

4.
The
\sigma
-algebra of invariant sets of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)

In this section the
\sigma
-algebra of invariant measurable sets of

((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)
will be studied. The main result of this section is that its
\sigma
-algebra of invariant measurable sets
is isomorphic to the
\sigma
-algebra of invariant measurable sets of its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactors.

4.1.
Multiple ergodic averages along polynomials of several variable

We need the following multiple ergodic averages along polynomials of several variable.

Theorem 4.1
.

[
31
, Theorem 3]

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
m,d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{d}:\mathbb{Z}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be non-constant essentially distinct polynomials (i.e.
p_{i}-p_{j}
is not constant for
i\neq j
). Then there is
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X)
with
\interleave f_{j}\interleave_{k}=0
for some
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
, one has

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{{\bf n}\in\Phi_{N}}T^{p_{1}({\bf n})}f_{1}\cdots T^{p_{d}({\bf n})}f_{d}=0

in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
for any Følner sequence
\{\Phi_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{m}
.

Remark 4.2
.

The integer
k
in Theorem
4.1
depends only on the weight of polynomials
\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
(see
[
31
]
for the definition of the weight and the proof of this fact).

4.2.
Condition
(\spadesuit)

For a given integral polynomial
p
with
p(0)=0
, for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
let
p^{[j]}
be defined by

p^{[j]}(n)=p(n+j)-p(j),\ \forall n\in\mathbb{Z}.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},p_{2},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of integral polynomials. We say
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
condition
(\spadesuit)
if
p_{1}(0)=\ldots=p_{d}(0)=0
and

(1)

p_{i}(n)=a_{i}n,1\leq i\leq s
, where
s\geq 0
, and
a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{s}
are distinct non-zero integers;

(2)

\deg p_{j}\geq 2,s+1\leq j\leq d
;

(3)

for each
i\neq j\in\{s+1,s+2,\ldots,d\}
,
p_{j}^{[k]}\neq p_{i}^{[t]}
for any
k,t\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 4.4
.

The key point in the definition of condition
(\spadesuit)
is the term (3). As we explained in the introduction, when we study
\mathcal{A}=\{p\}
with
p(n)=n^{2}
, we actually consider infinitely many polynomials

p^{[k]}
(
k\in\mathbb{Z}
) defined by
p^{[k]}(n)=p(n+k)-p(k)=n^{2}+2kn,n\in\mathbb{Z}
. But when we study
\mathcal{A}=\{n^{2},n^{2}+2n,n^{2}+8n\}
, we still consider the same polynomials
p^{[k]},k\in\mathbb{Z}
. To exclude this kind of situation, we add the term (3) when we study
\mathcal{A}
.

We have the following simple observation concerning the condition
(\spadesuit)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
Q=\{q_{1},q_{2},\ldots,q_{k}\}
be integral polynomials with
q_{i}(0)=0,1\leq i\leq k
. Then there is
1\leq d\leq k

and
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}\subset\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\}
such that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies condition
(\spadesuit)
, and
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\}\subseteq\{p_{i}^{[m]}:1\leq i\leq d,m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
.

Proof.

If there are
1\leq i\not=j\leq k
and
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
q_{i}^{[k_{1}]}=q_{j}^{[k_{2}]}
we just remove one of them (say
q_{j}
) to get a proper subset
Q^{\prime}=Q\setminus\{q_{j}\}
of
Q
. Continuing this process for finitely many steps, the argument will stop, and the resulting
\mathcal{A}
is what we want.
∎

Example 4.6
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\{n^{2},n^{2}+6n,n^{2}+10n\}
and
\mathcal{A}=\{n^{2}\}
. Then by the definition,
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
does not satisfy the condition
(\spadesuit)
,
\mathcal{A}
satisfies the condition
(\spadesuit)
and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\subseteq\{p^{[m]}:p(n)=n^{2}\in\mathcal{A},m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
. Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. Now we show that
(X^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
is isomorphic to
((X^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
.

Recall that

\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}=(T^{n^{2}}x)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in X^{\mathbb{Z}},\ \text{and}\ \omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}=\Big((T^{n^{2}}x,T^{n^{2}+6n}x,T^{n^{2}+10n}x)\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

Note that

\sigma^{k}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}=(T^{(n+k)^{2}}x)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=(T^{n^{2}+2kn+k^{2}}x)_{n\in Z}=(T^{\infty})^{k^{2}}(T^{n^{2}+2kn}x)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}},\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}.

We have
\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}=\Big(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}},(T^{\infty})^{-9}\sigma^{3}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}},(T^{\infty})^{-25}\sigma^{5}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}\Big)
for all
x\in X
.

Define
\Psi={\rm id}\times(T^{\infty})^{-9}\sigma^{3}\times(T^{\infty})^{-25}\sigma^{5}
:

\Psi:X^{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow(X^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}},\ {\bf x}\mapsto({\bf x},(T^{\infty})^{-9}\sigma^{3}{\bf x},(T^{\infty})^{-25}\sigma^{5}{\bf x}),\ \forall{\bf x}\in X^{\mathbb{Z}}.

It is easy to verify that

\Psi_{*}(\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})=\mu_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}^{(\infty)},

and
\Psi
is an isomorphism from
(X^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
to
((X^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{3})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
.

Similar to Example
4.6
, we have the following general result:

Theorem 4.7
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{k}\}
be a family of integral polynomials with
q_{i}(0)=0,1\leq i\leq k
. Then there is a family of integral polynomials
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
satisfying the condition
(\spadesuit)
such that

((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)\cong((X^{k})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{k})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},\sigma),

and

(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\cong(X^{t}\times(X^{k-t})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{t}\times(\mathcal{X}^{k-t})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},\widetilde{\sigma}),

where
t
is the number of linear polynomials in
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

4.3.
Some lemmas

Lemma 4.8

(van der Corput lemma)
.

[
2
]

Let
\{\zeta_{n}\}
be a bounded sequence in a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}

with norm
\parallel\cdot\parallel
and inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
. Then

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}\Big\|^{2}\leq\limsup_{H\to\infty}\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\langle\zeta_{n},\zeta_{n+h}\rangle\right|.

Recall that
\widetilde{\sigma}:X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}

is defined as follows: for

\big((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x}\big)\in X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},

\widetilde{\sigma}\Big((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x}\Big)=\Big(\tau_{\vec{a}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),\sigma_{II}{\bf x}\Big),

where
\tau_{\vec{a}}=T^{a_{1}}\times\cdots\times T^{a_{s}}
and
\sigma_{II}
is the shift on
(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

We now show

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying the condition
(\spadesuit)
.
Then for
all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
with
\interleave f_{i}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\interleave f_{j}^{(t)}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
, one has

(4.1)

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{s}f_{j}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\Big(\tau_{\vec{a}}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),\sigma_{II}^{n}{\bf x}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}=0.

Note that here
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
, and

\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{s}f_{j}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}
is regarded as an element of
L^{\infty}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{s},p_{s+1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials, where
s\geq 0
,
p_{i}(n)=a_{i}n
(
1\leq i\leq s
) satisfying
(\spadesuit)
.
Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
with
\interleave f_{i}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\interleave f_{j}^{(t)}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\|f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for all
i

and
\|f_{j}^{(t)}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for all
j,t
. Write

\begin{split}\zeta_{n}&=\Big(\big(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\Big(\tau_{\vec{a}}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),\sigma_{II}^{n}{\bf x}\Big)\\
&=f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x_{1})f_{2}(T^{a_{2}n}x_{2})\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x_{s})f_{-l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n-l})f_{-l+1}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n-l+1})\cdots f_{l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n+l})\\
&=f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x_{1})f_{2}(T^{a_{2}n}x_{2})\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x_{s})\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{(s+1)}(x^{(s+1)}_{n+j})f_{j}^{(s+2)}(x_{n+j}^{(s+2)})\cdots f_{j}^{(d)}(x_{n+j}^{(d)}),\end{split}

where
{\bf x}=({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=\Big((x^{(s+1)}_{n},x^{(s+2)}_{n},\ldots,x^{(d)}_{n})\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

By van der Corput lemma (Lemma
4.8
),

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}\leq\limsup_{H\to\infty}\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\overline{\zeta}_{n+h}\cdot\zeta_{n}d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\right|.

For simplicity, we only consider real-valued functions. Firstly we show two special cases, and then give the proof of the general case.

Case I
:
s=d
.

In this case all polynomials involved are linear and
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}={\mu}^{(d)}_{\vec{a}}
, where
\vec{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})
. The results follows from Lemma
2.5
.

Case II
:
s=0
.

In this case there are no linear items in
\mathcal{A}
and
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}={\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.
For
h>2l
, we have

\begin{split}&\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}{\zeta}_{n+h}\cdot\zeta_{n}d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\right|\\
&\quad=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}{f_{j}^{\otimes}}({\bf x}_{n+h+j})\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}({\bf x}_{n+j})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})\right|\\
&\quad=\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}({\bf x}_{n+j}){f_{j}^{\otimes}}({\bf x}_{n+h+j})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})\right|.\end{split}

By the definition of
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
,

(4.2)

\begin{split}&\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}({\bf x}_{n+j}){f_{j}^{\otimes}}({\bf x}_{n+h+j})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})\\
&\quad=\lim_{M\to\infty}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{X}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}(T^{\vec{p}(m+n+j)}x^{\otimes d}){f_{j}^{\otimes}}(T^{\vec{p}(m+n+h+j)}x^{\otimes d})d\mu(x)\\
&\quad=\lim_{M\to\infty}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{X}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{(1)}(T^{p_{1}(m+n+j)}x)f_{j}^{(2)}(T^{p_{2}(m+n+j)}x)\cdots f_{j}^{(d)}(T^{p_{d}(m+n+j)}x)\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad{f_{j}^{(1)}}(T^{p_{1}(m+n+h+j)}x){f_{j}^{(2)}}(T^{p_{2}(m+n+h+j)}x)\cdots{f_{j}^{(d)}}(T^{p_{d}(m+n+h+j)}x)d\mu(x).\end{split}

By (3) of the condition
(\spadesuit)
on
\mathcal{A}
, all
m
-polynomials

\{p_{i}(m+n+j),p_{i}(m+n+h+j),1\leq i\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l\}

are essentially distinct, and by Theorem
4.1
, the equation (
4.2
) is
0
.
Thus for
h>2l
,

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}{\zeta}_{n+h}\cdot\zeta_{n}d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\right|=0.

So by Lemma
4.8
,

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}\leq\limsup_{H\to\infty}\frac{1}{H}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}{\zeta}_{n+h}\cdot\zeta_{n}d{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\right|=0.

Case III
:
1\leq s\leq d-1
.

It is easy to see

\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}=\Big\langle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n},\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\Big\rangle=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{N-1}\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle.

For each
(n_{1},n_{2})\in[0,N-1]^{2}
with
|n_{1}-n_{2}|>2l
, we have that

(4.3)

\begin{split}&\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}{\zeta}_{n_{1}}\cdot\zeta_{n_{2}}d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\\
&\quad=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n_{1}}x_{1})\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n_{1}}x_{s})f_{-l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{1}-l})\ldots f_{l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{1}+l})\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n_{2}}x_{1})\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n_{2}}x_{s})f_{-l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{2}-l})\cdots f_{l}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{2}+l})d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\\
&\quad=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n_{1}}x_{i})f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n_{2}}x_{i})\Big)\Big(\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{1}+j})f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}({\bf x}_{n_{2}+j})\Big)d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\\
&\quad=\lim_{M\to\infty}\frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=0}^{M-1}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x),\end{split}

where

(4.4)

\begin{split}&B(n_{1},n_{2},m)\\
&=\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}(m+n_{1})}x)f_{i}(T^{a_{i}(m+n_{2})}x)\Big)\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{p_{s+1}(m+n_{1}+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(m+n_{1}+j)}x)\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{p_{s+1}(m+n_{2}+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(m+n_{2}+j)}x).\end{split}

For each
N\in\mathbb{N}
, by (
4.3
) we choose an
M(N)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\lim_{N\to\infty}M(N)=+\infty
and for each
(n_{1},n_{2})\in[0,N-1]^{2}
with
|n_{1}-n_{2}|>2l
, we have that

(4.5)

\begin{split}\Big|\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle-\frac{1}{M(N)}\sum_{m=0}^{M(N)-1}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x)\Big|<\frac{1}{N^{3}}.\end{split}

Let
\Phi_{N}=[0,N-1]^{2}\times[0,M(N)]
. Then
\{\Phi_{N}\}_{N=1}^{\infty}
is a
Følner sequence of
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
. Let
C_{l}^{N}=|\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in[0,N-1]^{2}:|n_{1}-n_{2}|\leq 2l\}|
. By (
4.5
)

(4.6)

\begin{split}&\ \ \ \big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{N-1}\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in[0,N-1]^{2}\setminus C_{l}^{N}}\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle+\frac{C_{l}^{N}}{N^{2}}\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in[0,N-1]^{2}\setminus C_{l}^{N}}\Big(\frac{1}{M(N)}\sum_{m=0}^{M(N)-1}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x)+\frac{1}{N^{3}}\Big)+\frac{C_{l}^{N}}{N^{2}}\\
&\quad\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in[0,N-1]^{2}}\frac{1}{M(N)}\sum_{m=0}^{M(N)-1}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x)+\frac{1}{N^{3}}+\frac{2C_{l}^{N}}{N^{2}}\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2},m)\in\Phi_{N}}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x)+\frac{1}{N^{3}}+\frac{2C_{l}^{N}}{N^{2}}.\end{split}

Since
\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{2C_{l}^{N}}{N^{2}}=0
, we have

(4.7)

\begin{split}&\limsup_{N\to\infty}\big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}=\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=0}^{N-1}\langle\zeta_{n_{1}},\zeta_{n_{2}}\rangle\\
&\leq\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2},m)\in\Phi_{N}}\int_{X}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)d\mu(x)\\
&\leq\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left\|\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2},m)\in\Phi_{N}}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)\right\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\\
&\leq\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left\|\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2},m)\in\Phi_{N}}B(n_{1},n_{2},m)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
&=\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{|\Phi_{N}|}\sum_{(n_{1},n_{2},m)\in\Phi_{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}(m+n_{1})}x)f_{i}(T^{a_{i}(m+n_{2})}x)\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{p_{s+1}(m+n_{1}+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(m+n_{1}+j)}x)\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{p_{s+1}(m+n_{2}+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(m+n_{2}+j)}x)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}.\end{split}

Recall that we assume
\interleave f_{i}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\interleave f_{j}^{(t)}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
s+1\leq t\leq d,1\leq j\leq l
. By the condition
(\spadesuit)
on
\mathcal{A}
, all
(n_{1},n_{2},m)
-polynomials

\{p_{t}(m+n_{1}+j),p_{t}(m+n_{2}+j),s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l\}

and
\{a_{i}(m+n_{1}),a_{i}(m+n_{2}):1\leq i\leq s\}
as polynomials of several variable
\mathbb{Z}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
are essentially distinct.
Thus by Theorem
4.1
, the last line in (
4.7
) is equal to
0
. So by (
4.7
),

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\zeta_{n}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}=0.

The whole proof is complete.
∎

The final lemma we need is the following

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
be the factor map from
X
to its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor. Then for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
), one has

(4.8)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big)\\
&\quad=\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{k}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big),\end{split}

where

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{\otimes_{II}}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\triangleq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(s+1)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(2)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(d)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}),

-l\leq j\leq l
, and
\widetilde{\sigma}=\tau_{\vec{a}}\times\sigma_{II}
.

Proof.

Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
f^{(s+1)}_{j},\cdots,f_{j}^{(d)}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
with
-l\leq j\leq l
. Since
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
is a standard measure,

\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}
is an element of
L^{\infty}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})
.

By the telescoping, it suffices to show that sum

(4.9)

\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\bigotimes_{t=s+1}^{d}f_{j}^{(t)}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big)=0

whenever
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{i}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})=0
for some
-l\leq j\leq l,s+1\leq t\leq d
, i.e.

\interleave f_{i}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\interleave f_{j}^{(t)}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
-l\leq j\leq l,s+1\leq t\leq d
.

By the Ergodic theorem we have

\begin{split}&\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\bigotimes_{t=s+1}^{d}f_{j}^{(t)}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}\\
&\quad=\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}\\
&\quad=\lim_{N\to\infty}\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big(\big(\bigotimes_{k=1}^{s}f_{k}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\Big(\tau_{\vec{a}}^{n}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),\sigma_{II}^{n}{\bf x}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})}\\
&\quad=0,\end{split}

where the last equality holds following from Lemma
4.9
since
\interleave f_{i}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
1\leq i\leq s
or
\interleave f_{j}^{(t)}\interleave_{\infty}=0
for some
-l\leq j\leq l,s+1\leq t\leq d
. This ends the proof.
∎

Now we are able to show

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
be the factor map from
X
to its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor.
Then the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is measurable with
respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

Every bounded function on
X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
which is measurable with respect
to
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
can be approximated in

L^{2}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)})
by finite sums of functions of the form

\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(f_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big),

where
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,

l\in\mathbb{N}
and
f^{(s+1)}_{j},\ldots,f_{j}^{(d)}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),-l\leq j\leq l
.
By Lemma
4.10
, one can assume that these functions are
measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}
. In this case

\big(f_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}
is measurable with respect to

\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Since this
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{Z}^{s}_{\infty}\times(\mathcal{Z}^{d-s}_{\infty})^{\mathbb{Z}}
is
invariant under
\widetilde{\sigma}=\tau_{\vec{a}}\times\sigma_{II}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}\Big(\big(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big|\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\Big)\\
&\quad=\lim\limits_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum\limits_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big(\big(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\circ\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\end{split}

is also measurable with respect to

\mathcal{Z}^{s}_{\infty}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Therefore
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is measurable with
respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.
∎

4.4.
The
\sigma
-algebra of invariant sets of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)

Before going on, we recall that when
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
is a factor of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
, we denote by the same letter the
\sigma
-algebra

\mathcal{Y}
and its inverse image by
\pi
,
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{Y})
. In other words, if
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
is a factor of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
, we think of
\mathcal{Y}
as a
sub-
\sigma
-algebra of
\mathcal{X}
.

Definition 4.12
.

Let
\pi:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
be a homomorphism.
\pi
is
ergodic

or
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
is an
ergodic extension
of
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
if
T
-invariant sets of
\mathcal{X}
is contained in
\mathcal{Y}
, i.e.
\mathcal{I}(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\subseteq\mathcal{Y}
.

By this definition, we can rephrase Proposition
4.11
as follows:

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
be the factor map from
X
to its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor. Then the factor map

\begin{split}\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}:\ &(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\longrightarrow(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\end{split}

is ergodic.

In particular, one has that the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is equal to (modulo isomorphisms) the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.11
, the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is measurable with
respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
. Thus, it means that
\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}
is ergodic by the definition.

Since
(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})
is the factor of

(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma}),

\mathcal{I}(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is contained in

\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
.
On the other hand, as

\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\subseteq\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},

we have that
\mathcal{I}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is contained in
\mathcal{I}(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
.
∎

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
.
Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
be the factor map from
X
to its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor, and let
\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{z}\ d\mu_{\infty}(z)
be the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
.
Then
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.
That is,

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\nu_{z_{1}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{s}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

We need to show that for
all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
), one has

\begin{split}&\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\\
&=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{1}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{s}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\end{split}

where

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{\otimes_{II}}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(s+1)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(2)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(d)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}),

-l\leq j\leq l
. But this follows from Lemma
4.10
by taking integrals both sides of (
4.8
).
∎

Since by (
3.17
) we have the isomorphism

\phi:((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)\cong(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma}),

we have the following commuting diagram

\textstyle{((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}}
\textstyle{(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}}
\textstyle{((Z_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mu_{\infty})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma}).}

By the diagram, we can replace
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
in results above by
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Thus, by Theorem
4.13
we have

Theorem 4.15
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
be the factor map from
X
to its
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor. Then the factor map

\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}:((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)\rightarrow((Z_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mu_{\infty})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)

is ergodic.

In particular, one has that the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
is equal to (modulo isomorphism) the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}((Z_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mu_{\infty})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
.

5.
Polynomial orbits in nilsystems

Now we work on pro-nilsystems. All results remain true for
\infty
-step pro-nilsystems.
Since
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)\cong(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma}),
we only give statements concering
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and the corresponding results for
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
can be proved similarly.

5.1.
The measure
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
on nilsystems

5.1.1.

Let
(X=G/\Gamma,\nu,T)
be a nilsystem with
T
the translation by an element
\tau\in G
.
Then
(X,T)
is a t.d.s. By Theorem
2.8
,
(X,T)
is uniquely ergodic if and only if
(X,\nu,T)
is ergodic if and only if
(X,T)
is minimal.
This result holds for pro-nilsystems.

5.1.2.

Now let
(X,T)
be a minimal topological pro-nilsystem (a minimal pro-nilsystem for short).
Let
x\in X
and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials with
p_{1}(0)=\ldots=p_{d}(0)=0
. Recall that

(5.1)

\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}=\Big((\tau^{p_{1}(n)}x,\tau^{p_{2}(n)}x,\ldots,\tau^{p_{d}(n)}x)\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

Note that
(\sigma\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x})(n)=(\tau^{p_{1}(n+1)}x,\tau^{p_{2}(n+1)}x,\ldots,\tau^{p_{d}(n+1)}x)
for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Recall that

(5.2)

N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})=\overline{\bigcup\{\sigma^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}:n\in\mathbb{Z},x\in X\}}\subseteq(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

In
[
26
]
we have shown the following result.

Theorem 5.1
.

[
26
, Theorem 5.5]

Let
(X,T)
be a minimal pro-nilsystem.
Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials with
p_{1}(0)=\cdots=p_{d}(0)=0
. Then we have

(1)

The system
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is a minimal pro-nilsystem.

(2)

For each
x\in X
, the system
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}},\sigma),\sigma)
is a
minimal pro-nilsystem.

Now we prove the following

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
(X,T)
be a minimal pro-nilsystem. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials. With the notations above, we have

(1)

(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is uniquely
ergodic with the
\infty
-joining
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

(2)

For each
x\in X
,
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x},\sigma),\sigma)
is
uniquely ergodic with some measure
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
.

(3)

\displaystyle\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)
.

(4)

For all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes}=f_{j}^{(1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(i)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),1\leq i\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
)

(5.3)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}(T^{\vec{p}(n+j)}x^{\otimes d})=\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}\Big)({\bf x})\ d\nu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}({\bf x}),\end{split}

for
\nu
a.e.
x\in X
.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.1
,
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is a minimal pro-nilsystem, and for each
x\in X
,
so is the system
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}},\sigma),\sigma)
. It follows by Theorem
2.8
that they are uniquely ergodic. We denote their unique measures by
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
and
\nu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}
respectively.

Now we show that (3) holds, that is,
\displaystyle\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)
.
Let

\varphi:X\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}),\ x\mapsto\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}.

Since the map

X\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}),\ x\mapsto\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}(\cdots\times T^{\vec{p}(n-1)}\times\underset{\bullet}{T^{\vec{p}(n)}}\times T^{\vec{p}(n+1)}\times\cdots)_{*}\delta_{x^{(\infty)}}

is continuous (here
x^{(\infty)}=(\ldots,x^{\otimes d},x^{\otimes d},\ldots)
) and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}(\cdots\times T^{\vec{p}(n-1)}\times\underset{\bullet}{T^{\vec{p}(n)}}\times T^{\vec{p}(n+1)}\times\cdots)_{*}\delta_{x^{(\infty)}}

converges to
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
weakly
\big(
as
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}},\sigma),\nu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
is uniquely ergodic
\big)
, we have that
\varphi
is a Borel map. Thus we can define a measure
\displaystyle\kappa=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)
. It is easy to check that
\kappa
is
\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle
-invariant. By (1), the system
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is uniquely ergodic with the
\infty
-joining
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and hence by the uniqueness
we have

\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\kappa=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x).

Since for each
x\in X
,
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x},\sigma),\sigma)
is
uniquely ergodic with the measure
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
, we have that
for all
F\in C((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})
,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}F(\sigma^{n}(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}))=\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}F({\bf x})\ d\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}({\bf x}),\ \ \text{where}\ {\bf x}=({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

That is, for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes}=f_{j}^{(1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(i)}_{j}\in C(X),1\leq i\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}(T^{\vec{p}(n+j)}x^{\otimes d})\\
&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{(1)}(T^{p_{1}(n+j)}x)f_{j}^{(2)}(T^{p_{2}(n+j)}x)\cdots f_{j}^{(d)}(T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&=\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}\Big)({\bf x})\ d\nu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}({\bf x}).\end{split}

Similar to the proof of Theorem
A.6
in the appendix, we may pass to bounded measurable functions.
The whole proof is now complete.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Note that though
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
is standard, for
x\in X
the measure
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
may not be standard.

5.1.3.

Similarly, we have a result related to
\widetilde{\nu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
(X,T)
be a minimal pro-nilsystem. Let
\mathcal{A}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials. With the notations above, we have

(1)

(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is uniquely
ergodic with the
\infty
-joining
\widetilde{\nu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

(2)

For each
x\in X
,
(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(\xi^{\mathcal{A}}_{x},\widetilde{\sigma}),\widetilde{\sigma})
is
uniquely ergodic with some measure
\widetilde{\nu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
.

(3)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\widetilde{\nu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)
.

(4)

For all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
),

(5.4)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&\quad=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})d\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)},\end{split}

for
\nu
a.e.
x\in X
,
where
((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})=((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})\in X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

5.2.
The ergodic decomposition

In Theorem
5.2
, we have proved that
\displaystyle\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)
and
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic under
\sigma
for each
x\in X
. In the rest of the section we study the ergodic decomposition of
\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
under
\sigma
.
First we recall some basic facts concerning the ergodic decomposition.

Theorem 5.5

(The ergodic decomposition)
.

[
19
, Theorem 8.7]

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be the
\sigma
-algebra of
T
-invariant sets. Let
\pi:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(W,{\mathcal{W}},\varsigma,T)
be the factor defined by
\mathcal{I}
so that
\mathcal{I}=\pi^{-1}({\mathcal{W}})
. Let
\mu=\int_{W}\mu_{w}d\varsigma(w)
be the disintegration of
\mu
over
\varsigma
and for every
w\in W
denote
X_{w}=\pi^{-1}(w)
and
\mathcal{X}_{w}=\mathcal{X}\cap X_{w}
, then:

(1)

T
acts as the identity
{\rm id}_{W}
on
W
.

(2)

For
\varsigma
a.e.
w\in W
, the system
(X_{w},\mathcal{X}_{w},\varsigma_{w},T|_{X_{w}})
is ergodic.

(3)

This decomposition is unique in the following sense:

If
(Z,\mathcal{Z},\vartheta)
is a probability space and
\psi:z\mapsto\widetilde{\mu}_{z}
a measurable map from
Z
into
{\mathcal{M}}_{T}^{erg}(X)
such that
\mu=\int_{Z}\widetilde{\mu}_{z}d\vartheta(z)
, then there exists a measurable map
\phi:(Z,\mathcal{Z},\vartheta)\rightarrow(W,{\mathcal{W}},\varsigma)
such that
\vartheta
-a.e.
z\in Z
,
\mu_{\phi(z)}=\widetilde{\mu}_{z}
.

Remark 5.6
.

(1)
For a m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
, a factor
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,T)
with a factor map
\pi:X\rightarrow Y
can be viewed as the
T
-invariant subalgebra
\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{Y})\subset\mathcal{X}
. One can also retrieve the factor
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,T)
as a measure-preserving transformation on the space
{\mathcal{M}}(X)
of Borel probability measures on
X
as follows. Disintegrate the measure
\mu
over
\nu
,
\mu=\int_{Y}\mu_{y}d\nu(y)
and observe that the
T
-invariance of
\mu
implies that
T_{*}\mu_{y}=\mu_{Ty}
for
\nu
-a.e.
y\in Y
. Let
\phi:Y\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}(X),\ y\mapsto\mu_{y}
and let
\kappa=\phi_{*}(\nu)
. Then
\phi:(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,T)\rightarrow({\mathcal{M}}(X),\kappa,T_{*})
is an isomorphism. In this case

\mu=\int_{Y}\mu_{y}d\nu(y)=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}(X)}\theta d\kappa(\theta),

which means for all
f\in C(X)
,

\int_{{\mathcal{M}}(X)}\left(\int_{X}f(x)d\theta(x)\right)d\kappa(\theta)=\int_{X}f(x)d\mu(x).

Thus the trivial one point factor is
(\{\mu\},\delta_{\mu})
, and

(X,\mu,T)\cong\Big(\{\delta_{x}:x\in X\},\mu,T_{*}\Big).

Refer to
[
19
, Chapter 8, Section 1]
for details.

(2) Now go back to Theorem
5.5
. For a t.d.s.
(X,T)
,
{\mathcal{M}}_{T}(X)
is a simplex and by Choquet’s theorem, for each
\mu\in M_{T}(X)
, there is a unique representation

\mu=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}^{erg}_{T}(X)}\theta d\kappa(\theta),

with
\kappa
a Borel probability measure on the
G_{\delta}
subset
{\mathcal{M}}^{erg}_{T}(X)
of
{\mathcal{M}}_{T}(X)
. And
(W,{\mathcal{W}},\varsigma,{\rm id})
is isomorphic to

({\mathcal{M}}(X),\kappa,T_{*})=\Big(\{\mu_{w}:w\in W\},\kappa,T_{*}\Big).

See the proof of
[
19
, Theorem 8.7]
for details.

5.3.
The ergodic decomposition of
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma

5.3.1.

Now consider a minimal pro-nilsystem
(X,T)
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials.
By Theorem
5.2
,
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
is a m.p.s.

Now we apply Theorem
5.5
to the system
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
. Let
(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)
be the factor of
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
defined by
\mathcal{I}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
, and let
\psi:(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)\rightarrow(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma,{\rm id})
be the corresponding factor map. Let

\displaystyle\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}]_{w}d\varsigma(w)

be the disintegration of
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
over
\varsigma
, and it is the ergodic decomposition of
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma
.

By Remark
5.6
, there is some measure
\kappa
on
{\mathcal{M}}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}))
such that

(W,\varsigma)\cong\Big({\mathcal{M}}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})),\kappa\Big)=\Big(\{[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}]_{w}:w\in W\},\kappa\Big),

and

(5.5)

\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}]_{w}d\varsigma(w)=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma}^{erg}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}))}\theta d\kappa(\theta).

In the proof of Theorem
5.2
, we have defined a Borel measurable map:

\varphi:X\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}^{erg}_{\sigma}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})),\ x\mapsto\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}.

Let
\widetilde{\kappa}=\varphi_{*}(\nu)
. Then combining with Theorem
5.2
, we have

(5.6)

\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\nu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\ d\nu(x)=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma}^{erg}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}))}\theta d\widetilde{\kappa}(\theta).

By (
5.5
), (
5.6
) and the uniqueness of the representation in Choquet’s theorem, we get
\widetilde{\kappa}=\kappa
which implies that
\nu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\varphi(x)\in\{[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}]_{w}:w\in W\}
. Thus we have the following Borel measurable map

\zeta:X\rightarrow W,\ x\mapsto\varphi(x)=[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}]_{w}\mapsto w.

To sum up, we have the following result:

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
(X,\nu,T)
be a minimal pro-nilsystem, and
\mathcal{A}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials. Let
(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)
be the factor of
(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
defined by
\mathcal{I}(N_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\sigma)
. Then there is a measure preserving map
\zeta:(X,\mathcal{X},\nu)\rightarrow(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)
such that

\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)

is the ergodic decomposition of
\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma
,
where
\big[\nu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\zeta(x)}=\nu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)},\ \mu\ a.e.\ x\in X.

5.3.2.

We have a similar result concerning
\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
(X,\nu,T)
be a minimal pro-nilsystem, and
\mathcal{A}
be a family of non-constant essentially distinct integral polynomials. Let
(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)
be the factor of
(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
defined by
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
. Then there is a measure preserving map
\zeta:(X,\mathcal{X},\nu)\rightarrow(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)
such that

\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}[\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)

is the ergodic decomposition of
\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\widetilde{\sigma}
,
where
\big[\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\zeta(x)}=\widetilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)},\ \mu\ a.e.\ x\in X.

6.
The ergodic decomposition of
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}

In this section, using the results in the previous sections, we show that for each family
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
and an ergodic m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
, the m.p.s.

((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)

is ergodic and give the ergodic decomposition theorem of
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma
. Moreover, we show that when each element in
\mathcal{A}
has degree greater than 1, then
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem
((Z_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
and a Bernoulli system
for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
.

6.1.
The ergodic decomposition theorem of
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma

6.1.1.

The main result of this section is the following one.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
with
p_{i}(n)=a_{i}n
,
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
0\leq i\leq s
; and
deg(p_{j})\geq 2
,
s+1\leq j\leq d
. Then there exists a family
\big\{\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\big\}_{x\in X}
of Borel probability measures on
(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

(1)

The
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
-m.p.s.
(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is ergodic.

(2)

The disintegrations of
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
over
\mu
is given by

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{X}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu(x),

and for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic under
\widetilde{\sigma}
;

(3)

For all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
),

(6.1)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&\quad=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}\end{split}

in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
, where
((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})=((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})\in X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into several steps.

Step 0. The results of Theorem
6.1
are independent of the choice of the topological models of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
.

First we show that Theorem
6.1
is independent of the topological models of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
which we choose. So, we need to show that all terms in Theorem
6.1
are isomorphic invariant.

Let
(X_{i},\mathcal{X}_{i},\mu_{i},T_{i}),i=1,2
, be two isomorphic ergodic m.p.s. and
\phi:(X_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1},\mu_{1},T)\rightarrow(X_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2},\mu_{2},T)

be the isomorphism. We assume that all results hold for
(X_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1},\mu_{1},T)
. That is, there exists a family
\big\{(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\big\}_{x\in X_{1}}
of Borel probability measures on
(X_{1}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

(1.1)

(X_{1}^{s}\times(X_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is ergodic.

(1.2)

The disintegrations of
(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
over
\mu_{1}
is as follows

(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{X_{1}}(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{1}(x),

and for
\mu_{1}
a.e.
x\in X_{1}
,
(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic under
\widetilde{\sigma}
;

(1.3)

For all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X_{1},\mu_{1})
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X_{1},\mu_{1})
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
),

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&\quad=\int_{X_{1}^{s}\times(X_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})d(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}\end{split}

in
L^{2}(X_{1},\mu_{1})
, where
((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})=((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})\in X_{1}^{s}\times(X_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Now we show that we have the same results for
(X_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2},\mu_{2},T)
.
Similar to the proof of Proposition
3.5
, we have that

\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty}:(X_{1}^{s}\times(X_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)\rightarrow(X_{2}\times(X_{2}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)

((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})\mapsto((\phi(x_{1}),\phi(x_{2}),\ldots,\phi(x_{s})),(\phi^{(d-s)}({\bf x}_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})

is an isomorphism and
(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
. Thus
((X_{2}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}_{2}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is ergodic.

For
\mu_{2}
a.e.
x\in X_{2}
, we define

(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}\big((\widetilde{\mu_{1}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\phi^{-1}(x)}\big).

Then
(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic and we have

\begin{split}(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}&=(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}\Big((\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\Big)\overset{(1.2)}{=}(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}\Big(\int_{X_{1}}(\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{1}(x)\Big)\\
&=\int_{X_{2}}(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}\big((\widetilde{\mu_{1}})_{\mathcal{A},\phi^{-1}(x)}^{(\infty)}\big)d\mu_{2}(x)=\int_{X_{2}}(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{2}(x).\end{split}

That is, we have that
\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{X_{2}}(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{2}(x)
.

Since
\phi:(X_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1},\mu_{1},T)\rightarrow(X_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2},\mu_{2},T)
is an isomorphism,
U_{\phi}:L^{2}(X_{2},\mu_{2})\rightarrow L^{2}(X_{1},\mu_{1}),

f\mapsto f\circ\phi
is an isometry. Thus,
for all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X_{2},\mu_{2})
,
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X_{2},\mu_{2}),s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
), we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)

converges in
L^{2}(X_{2},\mu_{2})
if and only if

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}\circ\phi(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}\circ\phi(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\circ\phi^{(d-s)}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)

converges in
L^{2}(X_{1},\mu_{1})
. And for
f\in L^{\infty}(X_{2},\mu)
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{X_{2}}f(x)\Big(\int_{X_{2}^{s}\times(X_{2}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})\ d(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\Big)d\mu_{2}(x)\\
&=\int_{X_{2}}f(x)\Big(\int_{X_{2}^{s}\times(X_{2}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\otimes\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})\ d(\phi^{(s)}\times\phi^{\infty})_{*}\big((\widetilde{\mu_{1}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\phi^{-1}(x)}\big)\Big)d\phi_{*}\mu_{1}(x)\\
&=\int_{X_{1}}f\circ\phi(x)\Big(\int_{X_{1}^{s}\times(X_{1}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\circ\phi\otimes\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\circ\phi^{(d-s)}\Big)((x_{1},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})\ d\big((\widetilde{\mu_{1}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\big)\Big)d\mu_{1}(x)\\
&\overset{(1.3)}{=}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{X_{1}}f\circ\phi(x)\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\circ\phi(T^{a_{i}n}x)\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\circ\phi^{(d-s)}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)d\mu_{1}(x)\\
\end{split}

which is equal to

\begin{split}=\int_{X_{2}}f(x)\left(\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n}x)\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\right)d\mu_{2}(x).\end{split}

Since
f
is arbitrary, we have

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}&\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n}x)\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&\overset{L^{2}(\mu_{2})}{\longrightarrow}\int_{X_{2}^{s}\times(X_{2}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})\ d(\widetilde{\mu_{2}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\ N\to\infty.\end{split}

Hence we have showed that all results in Theorem
6.1
are isomorphic invariant.

Step 1. Notation and the proof of (1).

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., and let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)

be the factor map, where
Z_{\infty}
is the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor of
X
.
Assume that
Z_{\infty}
is isomorphic to a topological
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem (see Theorem
2.14
, still denote it by
Z_{\infty}
). We build the uniquely ergodic minimal topological model
(\hat{X},\hat{T})
of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
in the following way
by Weiss’s theorem.

\begin{CD}X@>{}>{}>\hat{X}\\
@V{\pi_{\infty}}V{}V@V{}V{\hat{\pi}_{\infty}}V\\
Z_{\infty}@>{}>{}>Z_{\infty}\\
\end{CD}

Without loss of generality we assume that
(X,T)=(\hat{X},\hat{T})
and
\pi_{\infty}=\hat{\pi}_{\infty}
.

By Theorem
4.13
the factor map

\begin{split}\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}:\ &(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\longrightarrow(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times({Z_{\infty}^{d-s}})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\widetilde{\sigma})\end{split}

is ergodic.
And by Theorem
4.14
,
if
\displaystyle\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{z}\ d\mu_{\infty}(z)
is the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
, then
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.
That is,

(6.2)

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Since
{\rm supp}\ \widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})
and
{\rm supp}\ (\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})

(by Theorem
3.7
and the minimality of
(X,T)
and
(Z_{\infty},T)
), we may denote the factor map
\pi^{(s)}_{\infty}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty}
by

\pi^{(s)}_{\infty}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}:\Big(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma}\Big)\rightarrow\Big(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma}\Big).

By Theorem
4.13
,
\pi^{(s)}_{\infty}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty}
is ergodic, and
the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
is isomorphic to the
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
.
We denote the corresponding m.p.s. by

(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma,{\rm id})
. That is, we have

(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\overset{\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma,{\rm id})

so that

(6.3)

\begin{split}\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{W})=\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\cong\Phi^{-1}(\mathcal{W})=\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma}),\end{split}

where
\Phi=\phi\circ(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})
.

By Theorem
5.4
-(1),
(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is uniquely ergodic, which means that
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is trivial.

Now we show that
(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is ergodic. Let
A
be a
\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle
-invariant subset of
M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})
, i.e.
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A\Delta(T^{\infty})^{-1}A)=0,\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A\Delta\widetilde{\sigma}^{-1}A)=0
. Since
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A\Delta\widetilde{\sigma}^{-1}A)=0
, we have that
A\in\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
. By (
6.3
), there is some
B\in\mathcal{W}
and a measurable subset
A^{\prime}\subseteq M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})
such that
\phi^{-1}(B)=A^{\prime}
and
A=\Phi^{-1}(B)=(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})^{-1}(A^{\prime})
.
Now

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A^{\prime}\Delta(T^{\infty})^{-1}A^{\prime})=\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A\Delta(T^{\infty})^{-1}A)=0,

and

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A^{\prime}\Delta\widetilde{\sigma}^{-1}A^{\prime})=\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}(A\Delta\widetilde{\sigma}^{-1}A)=0.

As
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is trivial, we have
A^{\prime}=\emptyset
or
A^{\prime}=M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})\pmod{(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}
.
Thus
A=\emptyset
or
A=M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A})\pmod{\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is also trivial, and hence
(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\widetilde{\sigma}\rangle)
is ergodic. Thus (1) holds.

Step 2. The proof of (2).

By Step 1, we have

(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\overset{\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma,{\rm id})

so that

\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{W})=\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})=\Phi^{-1}(\mathcal{W})=\mathcal{I}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})
,
where
\Phi=\phi\circ(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})
.

By Theorem
5.5
, we have

(6.4)

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma}^{erg}(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}))}\theta d\kappa(\theta)

and

(6.5)

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{\sigma}^{erg}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}))}\theta d\widehat{\kappa}(\theta)

be the ergodic decomposition of
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
respectively, and one may assume that

(6.6)

\Phi_{*}\big(\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}\big)=\phi_{*}\big(\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}\big)=\delta_{w}\quad\varsigma\ a.e.\ w\in W.

By Remark
5.6
and (
6.3
),

(6.7)

\left(\big\{\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}:w\in W\big\},\kappa\right)\cong(W,\varsigma)\cong\left(\big\{\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}:w\in W\big\},\widehat{\kappa}\right)

By Theorem
5.8
, there is a measure preserving map

\zeta:(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty})\rightarrow(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma)

such that

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)=\int_{Z_{\infty}}(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},z}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{\infty}(z)

and

(6.8)

\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\zeta(z)}=(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},z}^{(\infty)},\ \mu_{\infty}\ a.e.\ z\in Z_{\infty}.

Let

\psi=\zeta\circ\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)\rightarrow(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma).

Then
\psi
is a measure preserving map. For
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
, define

(6.9)

\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\triangleq\big[\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}\big]_{\psi(x)}.

Then we have

(6.10)

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}&=\int_{W}\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w)=\int_{W}\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\psi_{*}\mu(w)\\
&=\int_{X}\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\psi(x)}d\mu(x)=\int_{X}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu(x),\end{split}

and for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic under
\widetilde{\sigma}
.

Step 3. For
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi(x)}^{(\infty)}
. That is, if
\displaystyle\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{z}\ d\mu_{\infty}(z)
is the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
, then

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\nu_{z_{1}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{s}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

By (
6.2
),
\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
.
That is,

(6.11)

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

By (
6.5
), we have

\begin{split}&\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&=\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&=\int_{W}\left(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\right)d\varsigma(w).\end{split}

On the other hand, by (
6.4
), we have

(6.12)

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{W}\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}d\varsigma(w).\end{split}

Recall that
(M_{\infty}(X,\mathcal{A}),\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\overset{\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}(M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A}),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\widetilde{\sigma})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}(W,\mathcal{W},\varsigma,{\rm id})
and
\Phi=\phi\circ(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})
.
We have that for
\varsigma
-a.e.
w\in W
,

\begin{split}&\Phi_{*}\Big(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\Big)\\
&=\phi_{*}\circ(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})_{*}\Big(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\Big)\\
&=\phi_{*}\Big(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi^{\infty}_{\infty})_{*}\Big(\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)\Big)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\Big)\\
&=\phi_{*}\Big(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\delta_{((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})}d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\Big)\\
&=\phi_{*}\Big(\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}\Big)\overset{\eqref{d8}}{=}\delta_{w}.\end{split}

Thus by (
6.6
), for
\varsigma
-a.e.
w\in W
,

\begin{split}&\Phi_{*}\Big(\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\Big)\\
&=\Phi_{*}\big(\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}\big)=\delta_{w}.\end{split}

By the uniqueness of the disintegration (
[
17
, Theorem 5.8]
or
[
19
, Theorem A.7]
), we have that for
\varsigma
-a.e.
w\in W
,

\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}=\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{w}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}).

Thus combined with (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), we have
for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,

\begin{split}&\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=\big[\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\psi(x)}\\
&=\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\psi(x)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&=\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d\big[(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\big]_{\phi(\pi_{\infty}(x))}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&=\int_{M_{\infty}(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{A})}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}).\end{split}

This ends the proof of Step 3.

Step 4. The proof of (3).

Now we show (3).
For all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
), we show

(6.13)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}\end{split}

in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
. Similar to the proof of Theorem
A.3
-(1), for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\ldots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\in L^{\infty}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)})
and the right side of the equation (
6.13
) makes sense. Again similar to the proof of Theorem
A.3
, we only need show (
6.13
) for continuous functions.

By Step 3, For
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}
. Thus for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{i},f^{(t)}_{j}\in C(X)
,
1\leq u\leq s,s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
,

(6.14)

\begin{split}&\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}\\
=&\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)\\
&\quad d\Big(\big(\nu_{z_{1}}\times\ldots\times\nu_{z_{s}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
=&\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\int_{X}f_{1}d\nu_{z_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\int_{X}f_{s}d\nu_{z_{s}}\Big)\otimes\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\prod_{t=s+1}^{d}\int_{X}f_{j}^{(t)}d\nu_{z_{n}^{(t)}}d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
=&\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{1}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{s}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)},\end{split}

where
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{\otimes_{II}}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(s+1)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(2)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f^{(d)}_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}),-l\leq j\leq l.

We need to explain the last equality. Since
\displaystyle\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{z}\ d\mu_{\infty}(z)
is the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
, we have that for every
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, there is some subset
A_{f}\in\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}
with
\mu_{\infty}(A_{f})=1
such that
f
is in
L^{\infty}(X,\nu_{z})
, and
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})(z)=\int_{X}fd\nu_{z}
for each
z\in A_{f}
.
Since
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
is a standard measure on
Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have that

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}\left((\prod_{i=1}^{s}A_{f_{i}})\times\Big(\big(\prod_{j=-\infty}^{-l-1}Z_{\infty}^{d-s}\big)\times\big(\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\prod_{t=s+1}^{d}A_{f^{(t)}_{j}}\big)\times\big(\prod_{j=l+1}^{\infty}Z_{\infty}^{d-s}\big)\Big)\right)=1.

Since
\displaystyle(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z_{\infty}}(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},z}^{(\infty)}d\mu_{\infty}(z)
, there is some
B\in\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}
with
\mu_{\infty}(B)=1
such that for all
z\in B
, we have

(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},z}^{(\infty)}\left((\prod_{i=1}^{s}A_{f_{i}})\times\Big(\big(\prod_{j=-\infty}^{-l-1}Z_{\infty}^{d-s}\big)\times\big(\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\prod_{t=s+1}^{d}A_{f^{(t)}_{j}}\big)\times\big(\prod_{j=l+1}^{\infty}Z_{\infty}^{d-s}\big)\Big)\right)=1.

Thus for each
z\in B
and for
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},z}^{(\infty)}
-a.e. all
((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\begin{split}&\Big(\int_{X}f_{1}d\nu_{z_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\int_{X}f_{s}d\nu_{z_{s}}\Big)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\prod_{t=s+1}^{d}\int_{X}f_{j}^{(t)}d\nu_{z_{n}^{(t)}}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{1}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{s}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}).\end{split}
