And the last equality of (
6.14
) holds for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
.

By Theorem
5.4
, in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
we have

\begin{split}&\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}\\
=&\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{1}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{s}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}\\
\overset{L^{2}(\mu)}{=}&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{i=1}^{s}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{i}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})(T^{a_{i}n}\pi_{\infty}(x))\cdot\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}\pi_{\infty}(x),\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}\pi_{\infty}(x))\\
=&\lim_{N\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x).\end{split}

The last equality follows from Lemma
4.9
.
The whole proof is complete.
∎

Since the Step 3 of the proof is important in the sequel, we restate it here.

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\pi_{\infty}
be factor map from
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
to
(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
, where
Z_{\infty}
is the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor of
X
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Let
\big\{\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\big\}_{x\in X}
be the family of Borel probability measures on
(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
in Theorem
6.1
. Then for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}
. That is, if
\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{s}\ d\mu_{\infty}(s)
be the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
, then

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
. In particular,

(\pi_{\infty}^{(s)}\times\pi_{\infty}^{\infty})_{*}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}=(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}.

We can give a similar result for
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
as for
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
stated in Theorem
6.1
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Then there exists a family
\big\{\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\big\}_{x\in X}
of Borel probability measures on
(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

(1)

((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)},\langle T^{\infty},\sigma\rangle)
is ergodic.

(2)

The disintegrations of
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
over
\mu
is given by
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}=\int_{X}\mu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}d\mu(x).

And for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is ergodic under
\sigma
;

(3)

For all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes}=f_{j}^{(1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(i)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),1\leq i\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
)

(6.15)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}(T^{\vec{p}(n+j)}x^{\otimes d})\overset{L^{2}(\mu)}{\longrightarrow}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}\Big)({\bf x})d\mu_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}({\bf x}),\ N\to\infty,\end{split}

where
{\bf x}=({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Note that when
s=0
, i.e. all polynomials in
\mathcal{A}
are non-linear, then Theorem
6.1
and Theorem
6.3
are the same.

6.2.
The structure of
\big(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\widetilde{\sigma}\big)

We can give an explicit structure of
\big(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\widetilde{\sigma}\big)
for almost all
x\in X
by using Corollary
6.2
. Since we need Lemma
4.9
in the proof, we do not have a nice corresponding result for
{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
.

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be ergodic and
\pi
be the factor map from
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
to
(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
, where
Z_{\infty}
is the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor of
X
. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Then there exist a probability space
(U,{\mathcal{U}},\rho)
and a measurable cocycle
\alpha:Z_{\infty}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(U,\rho)
, and there is a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that
for each
x\in X_{0}
,
\big(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{s}\times(\mathcal{X}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\widetilde{\sigma}\big)
is isomorphic to
\Big(\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big),\big(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{s}\times(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big({\mathcal{U}}^{s}\times({\mathcal{U}}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big),(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times\big(\rho^{s}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big),\widehat{\sigma}\Big)
,
where

\widehat{\sigma}:\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\rightarrow\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)

is defined as follows:

(6.16)

\begin{split}&\widehat{\sigma}\big(((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),((u_{1},\ldots,u_{s}),{\bf u})\big)\\
&\quad=\big(\widetilde{\sigma}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),((\alpha(z_{1})u_{1},\ldots,\alpha(z_{s})u_{s}),\sigma{\bf u})\big),\end{split}

where
\big(((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),((u_{1},\ldots,u_{s}),{\bf u})\big)\in\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)
and
\sigma:(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
is the shift.

Proof.

Let
\pi
be the factor map from
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
to
(Z_{\infty},\mathcal{Z}_{\infty},\mu_{\infty},T)
. By Rohlin’s skew-product theorem (Theorem
2.1
), we may assume that there exist a probability pace
(U,{\mathcal{U}},\rho)
and a measurable cocycle
\alpha:Z_{\infty}\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(U,\rho)
such that

(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)=(Z_{\infty}\times U,\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}\times{\mathcal{U}},\mu_{\infty}\times\rho,T_{\alpha}),

where
T_{\alpha}(y,u)=(Ty,\alpha(y)u)
.
In this case
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})(y,u)=\int_{U}f(y,u)d\rho(u)
for all
f\in L^{1}(X,\mu)
.
We assume that
X,Z_{\infty}
and
U
are all compact metric spaces.

Define

\Psi:X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)

\Big(\big((z_{1},u_{1}),\ldots,(z_{s},u_{s})\big),{\bf x}\Big)\mapsto\big(((z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),((u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{s}),{\bf u})\big),

where
{\bf x}=\big((z_{n}^{(s+1)},u_{n}^{(s+1)}),\ldots,(z_{n}^{(d)},u_{n}^{(d)})\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
{\bf z}=(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
{\bf u}=(u_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,u_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Via the map
\Psi
, we may regard
\big(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}},\widetilde{\sigma}\big)
as
\Big(\big(Z_{\infty}^{s}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\times\big(U^{s}\times(U^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big),\widehat{\sigma}\Big)
.
Now we show that for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\cong(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times\big(\rho^{s}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)
.

By Corollary
6.2
, there is a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that
for each
x\in X_{0}
,
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
is the conditional product measure with
respect to
(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}
. That is, if
\mu=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\nu_{z}\ d\mu_{\infty}(z)
be the disintegration of
\mu
over
\mu_{\infty}
, then

\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}),

where
\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z}\big)=\big((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),(z_{n}^{(s+1)},\ldots,z_{n}^{(d)})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)\in Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Now
\displaystyle\mu=\mu_{\infty}\times\rho=\int_{Z_{\infty}}\delta_{z}\times\rho d\mu_{\infty}(z)
, i.e.
\nu_{z}=\delta_{z}\times\rho
for
\mu_{\infty}
-a.e.
z\in Z_{\infty}
. It follows that
for
x\in X_{0}

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}&=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\prod_{i=1}^{s}\nu_{z_{i}}\big)\times\prod_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\nu_{z_{n}^{(s+1)}}\times\cdots\times\nu_{z_{n}^{(d)}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&=\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(\delta_{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{s})}\times\rho^{s}\big)\times\Big(\delta_{{\bf z}}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\Big)d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\\
&\cong\left(\int_{Z^{s}_{\infty}\times(Z_{\infty}^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\delta_{(z_{1},\ldots,z_{s})}\times\delta_{{\bf z}}d(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}((z_{1},\ldots,z_{s}),{\bf z})\right)\times\big(\rho^{s}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\\
&=(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times\big(\rho^{s}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big).\end{split}

The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

We have

(1)

When
s=d
, each element in
\mathcal{A}
is linear and in Theorem
6.4
we have
\mu^{(d)}_{\vec{a},x}\cong(\mu_{\infty})^{(d)}_{\vec{a},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times\rho^{d}
, where
\vec{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})
.

(2)

When
s=0
, each element in
\mathcal{A}
has degree greater than 1. Hence
\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
. By Theorem
6.4
, for
x\in X_{0}

{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\cong(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}=({\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

That is,
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem
\big((Z_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)},\sigma\big)
and a Bernoulli system
((U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},({\mathcal{U}}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\sigma)
.

We now derive some consequences of the above results.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., where
(X,T)
is a t.d.s. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{d}\}
be a family of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Then there is a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that
there is some increasing subsequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
such that in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}})
, for all
x\in X_{0}
,

\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{i}}\delta_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\quad i\to\infty.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.1
, for all
F\in C(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}})
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}F(\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}(\xi_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}))\overset{L^{2}(\mu)}{\longrightarrow}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}F\big(((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})\big)\ d\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}(((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})),

where
((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x})=((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),({\bf x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}})\in X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a countable dense subset in
C(X)
. Since mean convergence of a sequence of functions implies pointwise almost everywhere convergence along a subsequence, we can find a subsequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
with
N_{i}\to\infty
such that
there is some measurable set
X_{0}\subseteq X
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
satisfying that for
x\in X_{0}

and for all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in{\mathcal{F}}
, and for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
(where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in{\mathcal{F}}
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
),

\begin{split}&\quad\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\\
&=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{s}\big)\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}\Big)({\bf x})d\widetilde{\mu}_{\mathcal{A},x}^{(\infty)}((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{s}),{\bf x}).\end{split}

That is, for all
x\in X_{0}
,

\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\delta_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}},\ w^{*}\ \text{in ${\mathcal{M}}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}})$}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

We have

(1)

If in Theorem
6.1
-(3), the equation (
6.1
) holds almost surely, then in Theorem
6.6
,
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is
\{N\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, that is,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\delta_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}=\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}\cong(\widetilde{\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times\big(\rho^{s}\times(\rho^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}}\big).

(2)

By the same proof, the statement of Theorem
6.6
can be replaced by the following: for any increasing sequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
, there is an increasing subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}})
, for all
x\in X_{0}
,

\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{i}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{i}}\delta_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\quad i\to\infty.

7.
Convergence theorems

In this section we will give some applications of the theory we developed in the previous sections,
namely we will show some mean and pointwise convergence results, which extend the corresponding ones proved in
[
14
]
. The general ideas of the proofs are similar to the ones used in
[
14
]
. The essential difficulty we face is that
several basic facts used in the proof of
[
14
]
are only stated for a single term
f(T^{n})
. To show our results we need to prove
the facts for the multiple terms
f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n})\cdots f_{m}(T^{a_{m}n})
and
g_{1}(S^{p_{1}})\cdots g_{d}(S^{p_{d}})
, which are done either in the
previous subsections (for example Remark
6.7
) or in this section and the appendix (for example Theorem
7.6
).

7.1.
Equivalent conditions for pointwise convergence of multiple ergodic averages

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s. Then the following statements are equivalent.

(1)

For all
d\in\mathbb{N}
, families
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
of non-constant integral polynomials satisfying
(\spadesuit)
, for any
F\in C(X^{s}\times(X^{d-s})^{\mathbb{Z}})
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}F(\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})

converges
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
.

(2)

For all
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{s},p_{s+1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
satisfying
(\spadesuit)
, where
s\geq 0
,
p_{i}(n)=a_{i}n
,
1\leq i\leq s
,
a_{1},\ldots,a_{s}
are distinct non-zero integers, and
\deg p_{i}\geq 2,s+1\leq i\leq d
, for all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, and for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}=f_{j}^{(s+1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(s+2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}
, (where
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
s+1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
),

\begin{split}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes_{II}}(T^{{p_{s+1}}(n+j)}x,\ldots,T^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\end{split}

converges almost surely.

(3)

For all
d\in\mathbb{N}
, non-constant integral polynomials
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{p_{1}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{p_{d}(n)}x)

converges almost surely.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
A.6
one can prove that (1) is equivalent to (2). It is clear that (3) implies (2). It remains to show (2) implies (3).

Without loss of generality, we assume that
p_{1},p_{2},\cdots,p_{d}
are essentially distinct.
Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1}(n)=a_{1}n,\ldots,p_{s}(n)=a_{s}n,p_{s+1}(n),\ldots,p_{d}(n)\}
. If
\mathcal{A}
satisfies the condition (
\spadesuit
), then by (2) (in this case we take
l=0
), we have

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{p_{1}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{p_{d}(n)}x)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{s}(T^{a_{s}n}x)f_{s+1}(T^{p_{s+1}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{p_{d}(n)}x)

converge almost surely.

If
\mathcal{A}
does not satisfy the condition (
\spadesuit
), then
by Lemma
4.5
, there are integral polynomials
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{r}\}
satisfying the condition (
\spadesuit
) and
\{p_{s+1},\ldots,p_{d}\}\subseteq\{q_{i}^{[j]}=q_{i}(n+j)-q_{i}(j):1\leq i\leq r,j\in\mathbb{Z}\}.
Thus there is some
l\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\{p_{s+1},\ldots,p_{d}\}\subseteq\{q_{i}^{[j]}=q_{i}(n+j)-q_{i}(j):1\leq i\leq r,-l\leq j\leq l\}.

Then
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\{a_{1}n,\ldots,a_{s}n,q_{1}(n),\ldots,q_{r}(n)\}
satisfies the condition (
\spadesuit
).

Applying (2) to
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and
l
, we have
for all
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{s}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, and for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f^{(t)}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,
1\leq t\leq r,1\leq j\leq l
,

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{i=1}^{s}f_{i}(T^{a_{i}n}x)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{l}f_{j}^{(1)}(T^{q_{1}(n+j)}x)\cdots f_{j}^{(r)}(T^{q_{r}(n+j)}x)

converges
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
. Note that

\{p_{s+1},\ldots,p_{d}\}\subseteq\{q_{i}^{[j]}=q_{i}(n+j)-q_{i}(j):1\leq i\leq r,-l\leq j\leq l\}.

It follows that for all
f_{1},\cdots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{p_{1}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{p_{d}(n)}x)

converge almost surely. The proof is complete.
∎

7.2.
Mean convergence

First we need some preparations.

7.2.1.
Pinsker factor

Every m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
admits a largest factor belonging to
the class of systems of entropy zero. This factor is called the
Pinsker factor
of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)

and is denoted by
\Pi(X,T)
.
It is well known that the Pinsker factor of the product of two m.p.s. is the product of their Pinsker factors (see for example
[
37
, Chapter 4, Theorem 14]
or
[
19
, Theorem 18.13.]
), i.e. for m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
and
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
, we have

\Pi(X\times Y,T\times S)=\Pi(X,T)\times\Pi(Y,S).

By this result, we have the following observation.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. with zero entropy and let
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
be a Bornoulli system. Then
\Pi(X\times Y,T\times S)=\mathcal{X}\times\{Y,\emptyset\}
, i.e.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
is the Pinsker factor of
(X\times Y,\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y},\mu\times\nu,T\times S)
.

The following result is from
[
40
]
(see also
[
9
,
32
]
).

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\lambda
be a joining of a m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
with zero entropy and a m.p.s.
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
. Let
f\in L^{2}(X,\mu)
and
g\in L^{2}(Y,\nu)
. If
\mathbb{E}_{\nu}(g|\Pi(Y,S))=0
, then

\int_{X\times Y}f(x)g(y)d\lambda(x,y)=0.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. with zero entropy and let
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
. Let
\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c}}
be the Furstenberg joining respect to
\vec{c}=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{m})
. Then there is an
X^{*}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X^{*})=1
such that for all
x\in X^{*}
,
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy.

Proof.

Since
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
has zero entropy, for all
c\in\mathbb{Z}
,
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T^{c})
has zero entropy.
Thus as a joining of
\{(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T^{c_{i}})\}_{i=1}^{m}
, the m.p.s.
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c}},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy, i.e.
h_{\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}}(X^{d},\tau_{\vec{c}})=0
.

By Theorem
2.3
, the disintegrations of
\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}
over
\mu
is
\displaystyle\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}=\int_{X}\mu_{\vec{c},x}^{(m)}d\mu(x),
and for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
,
\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x}
is ergodic under
\tau_{\vec{c}}=T^{c_{1}}\times T^{c_{2}}\times\cdots\times T^{c_{m}}
.

Since
\displaystyle\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}=\int_{X}\mu_{\vec{c},x}^{(m)}d\mu(x),
we have (see
[
19
, Theorem 15.12]
)

(7.1)

h_{\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}}(X^{m},\tau_{\vec{c}})=\int_{X}h_{\mu_{\vec{c},x}^{(m)}}(X^{m},\tau_{\vec{c}})d\mu(x).

As
h_{\mu_{\vec{c}}^{(m)}}(X^{m},\tau_{\vec{c}})=0
, it follows that there is an
X^{*}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X^{*})=1
such that for all
x\in X^{*}
,
h_{\mu_{\vec{c},x}^{(m)}}(X^{m},\tau_{\vec{c}})=0
.
The proof is complete.
∎

7.2.2.
A convergence theorem

The following result is a convergence theorem appearing in
[
8
]
, which is a direct consequence of a generalization of Wiener-Wintner theorem proved by Host-Kra
[
22
]
(see Theorem
7.10
).

Theorem 7.5
.

[
8
, Corollary 6.3]

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
(\phi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a uniformly bounded sequence of
\mu
-measurable functions such that for
\mu
-almost every
x\in X
, the sequence
(\phi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a
k
-step nilsequence for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the averages

\frac{1}{N-M}\sum_{n=M}^{N-1}\phi_{n}(x)f(T^{n}x)

converge in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
as
N-M\to\infty
.

If in addition
\interleave f\interleave_{k+1}=0
, then
\displaystyle\frac{1}{N-M}\sum_{n=M}^{N-1}\phi_{n}(x)f(T^{n}x)
converge to
0
in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
.

We need a generalization of the above theorem, which will be proved in the appendix (see Theorem
B.4
for a more general result).

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
(\phi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a uniformly bounded sequence of
\mu
-measurable functions such that, for
\mu
-almost every
x\in X
, the sequence
(\phi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a
k
-step nilsequence. Then for all
c_{1},\ldots,c_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}
and for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the averages

\frac{1}{N-M}\sum_{n=M}^{N-1}\phi_{n}(x)f_{1}(T^{c_{1}n}x)f_{2}(T^{c_{2}n}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{c_{d}n}x)

converge in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
as
N-M\to\infty
.

7.2.3.

Now we start to prove convergence theorems. First we show the following

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
T,S
be ergodic measure preserving transformations acting on a probability space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
such that
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
has zero entropy. Let
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
and
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
be non-constant integral polynomials with
\deg{p_{i}}\geq 2,1\leq i\leq d
satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. Then for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
f_{i},g_{j}^{(t)}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),1\leq i\leq m,1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
t\in[1,d],j\in[-l,l]
, one has

(7.2)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\Big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\Big)=0

in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty}(S),\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S),\mu_{\infty},S)

be the factor map, where
Z_{\infty}(S)
is the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)
.
By Theorem
2.1
, we may assume that there exist a probability pace
(U,{\mathcal{U}},\rho)
and a measurable cocycle
\alpha:Z_{\infty}(S)\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(U,\rho)
such that

(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)=(Z_{\infty}(S)\times U,\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)\times{\mathcal{U}},\mu_{\infty}\times\rho,S_{\alpha}).

In this case
\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty})(y,u)=\int_{U}f(y,u)d\rho(u)
for all
f\in L^{1}(X,\mu)
.
As usual, we assume that
X,Z_{\infty}(S)
and
U
are all compact metric spaces.

First we show that it suffices to show (
7.2
) holds for all continuous functions. To see this, first note that in
L^{1}(X,\mu)
the set
\{g\in C(X):\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0\}
is dense in
\{f\in L^{1}(X,\mu):\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0\}
, i.e. for each
f\in L^{1}(X,\mu)
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
and
\epsilon>0
, there is some
g\in C(X)
such that
\|f-g\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\leq\epsilon
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
. In fact, for each
f\in L^{1}(X,\mu)
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
and
\epsilon>0
, choose
\widetilde{g}\in C(X)
such that
\|f-\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}<\frac{\epsilon}{2}
. Let
g(y,u)=\widetilde{g}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\widetilde{g}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=\widetilde{g}(y,u)-\int_{U}\widetilde{g}(y,u)d\rho(u)
. Then
g\in C(X)
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
, and

\begin{split}\|f-g\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}&=\big\|(f-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)))-(\widetilde{g}-\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(\widetilde{g}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)))\big\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\\
&\leq\|f-\widetilde{g}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}+\|\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(f-\widetilde{g}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\leq\epsilon.\end{split}

Next similar to the proof of Theorem
A.3
, one can show that if (
7.2
) holds for all continuous functions, then (
7.2
) holds for all bounded measurable functions. Thus it suffices to show (
7.2
) holds for all continuous functions.

Arguing by contradiction, suppose that the conclusion fails. Then there are some
l\in\mathbb{N}
,
f_{i},g_{j}^{(t)}\in C(X),1\leq i\leq m,1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
t\in[1,d],j\in[-l,l]
, one has

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\Big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\Big)=0

does not hold in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
.
Then there exist
\epsilon>0
and
N_{i}\to\infty
such that

(7.3)

\left\|\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\Big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\Big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\geq\epsilon

for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

By Remark
6.7
-(3), there is a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
and subsequence
\{N_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
x\in X_{0}
,
in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{m})
and
{\mathcal{M}}((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})

\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}^{\prime}-1}\delta_{\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m}}=\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x}\quad\text{and }\quad\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}^{\prime}-1}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}={\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},

where
\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}=\big(S^{\vec{p}(n)}x^{\otimes d}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
S^{\vec{p}(n)}=S^{p_{1}(n)}\times\cdots\times S^{p_{d}(n)}
.

By Theorem
6.4
,

{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=({\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},

and
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of a
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem

((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)

and a Bernoulli system
((U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},({\mathcal{U}}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\sigma)
.

By Lemma
7.4
, there is an
X^{*}\in\mathcal{X}
with a full measure
\mu(X^{*})=1
such that for all
x\in X^{*}
,
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy.

By the proof of the last equality of (
6.14
) in Theorem
6.1
, there is some
X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X^{\prime})=1
such that for each
x\in X^{\prime}
and for
({\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}
-a.e.
{\bf z}\in(Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,

(7.4)

\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\int_{U}g_{j}^{(1)}d\rho\cdot\cdots\cdot\int_{U}g_{j}^{(d)}d\rho\big)=\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(1)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\cdot\cdots\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(d)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\big).

Now let
x\in X_{0}\cap X^{*}\cap X^{\prime}
, and let
\lambda_{x}
be a limit point of the following sequence in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})

\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}^{\prime}-1}\delta_{(\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m},\sigma^{n}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})}\Big\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}.

That is, there is some subsequence
\{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\{N_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(7.5)

\lambda_{x}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}-1}\delta_{(\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m},\sigma^{n}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})},\quad\text{weak${}^{*}$ in ${\mathcal{M}}(X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})$}.

Thus
\lambda_{x}
is a joining of
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
and
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
.

Since
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem

((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)

and a Bernoulli system
((U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},({\mathcal{U}}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\sigma)
, by Lemma
7.2
the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor
((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)},\sigma)
is also
the Pinsker factor of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
.

Since
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
t\in\{1,2,\ldots,d\},j\in\{-l,-l+1,\ldots,l\}
and
{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=({\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

(7.6)

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)|(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}\Big)\\
&=\int_{(Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\int_{(U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)d(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}\Big)d{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\\
&=\int_{(Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\int_{U}g_{j}^{(1)}d\rho\cdot\cdots\cdot\int_{U}g_{j}^{(d)}d\rho\big)\Big)d{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\\
\end{split}

which is equal to

\begin{split}\overset{\eqref{b2}}{=}\int_{(Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(1)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\cdot\cdots\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(d)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\big)\Big)d{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}=0.\end{split}

Since
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy and
((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)},\sigma)
is the Pinsker factor of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
, by Theorem
7.3
and (
7.2.3
), one has

(7.7)

\int_{X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)d\lambda_{x}=0.

By (
7.5
), for all
x\in X_{0}\cap X^{*}\cap X^{\prime}
,

\begin{split}&\quad\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}-1}f_{1}(T^{c_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{c_{m}n}x)\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}x)\cdots g_{j}^{(d)}(S^{p_{d}(n+j)}x)\big)\\
&=\int_{X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)d\lambda_{x}\overset{\eqref{j4}}{=}0.\end{split}

Since
\{N_{i}^{\prime\prime}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is a subsequence of
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mu(X_{0}\cap X^{*}\cap X^{\prime})=1
, the equation above contradicts with (
7.3
).
The proof is complete.
∎

Now it is time to prove the main result of this subsection.

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
T,S
be ergodic measure preserving transformations acting on a probability space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
such that
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
has zero entropy. Let
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}
and
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
be non-constant integral polynomials with
\deg{p_{i}}\geq 2,1\leq i\leq d
. Then for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m},g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the limit

(7.8)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot S^{p_{1}(n)}g_{1}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n)}g_{d}

exists in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
.

Proof.

We assume that the family
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
satisfies
(\spadesuit)
. If not, one can handle the proof using the analysis in the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Since
\mathcal{A}=\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}\}
satisfies
(\spadesuit)
, by Proposition
7.7
we have

\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot S^{p_{1}(n)}g_{1}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n)}g_{d}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0,

whenever
\mathbb{E}(g_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
.
Thus we can restrict to the case where
g_{j},1\leq j\leq d
, is measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)
and using an
L^{2}(\mu)
approximation argument we can assume that it is measurable with respect to the factor
\mathcal{Z}_{k}(S)
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

By Theorem
2.17
, for every
\epsilon>0
there exists
\widetilde{g}_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
such that
\widetilde{g}_{j}
is
\mathcal{Z}_{k}(S)
-measurable and
\|g_{j}-\widetilde{g}_{j}\|_{L^{1}(X,\mu)}<\epsilon,1\leq j\leq d
, and for
\mu
-almost all
x\in X
the sequence

(\widetilde{g}_{j}(S^{n}x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a
k
-step nilsequence.
By
[
31
, Proposition 3.14]
or
[
23
, Theorem 14.15]
, for
\mu
-almost all
x\in X
the sequence
(\widetilde{g}_{j}(S^{p_{j}(n)}x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a
(k\deg{p_{j}})
-step nilsequence for
1\leq j\leq d
. Let
X_{1}\in\mathcal{X}
be a full measure subset of
X
for which
this property holds.
Thus
(\Psi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{d}\widetilde{g}_{j}(S^{p_{j}(n)}x)\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a nilsequence.
Therefore, it suffices to show the existence in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
of the limit

(7.9)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi_{n}(x)f_{1}(T^{c_{1}n}x)f_{2}(T^{c_{2}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{c_{m}n}x)

This follows from Theorem
7.6
. The proof is complete.
∎

7.3.
Pointwise convergence

In this subsection we give some pointwise convergence results assuming some natural conjectures.

7.3.1.
The main result

Before presenting the result we state two natural conjectures. First we recall the following well known result of
Bourgain.

Theorem 7.9

(Bourgain’s double recurrence theorem)
.

[
7
]

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then for all
f,g\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
the averages

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f(T^{c_{1}n}x)g(T^{c_{2}n}x)

converge almost surely.

In general, one has the following well-known conjecture.

Conjecture 1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for all non-constant integral polynomials
p_{1},\cdots,p_{d}
and
f_{1},\cdots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the averages

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{p_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T^{p_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{p_{d}(n)}x)

converge almost surely.

For the recent progress along the conjecture, see
[
35
]
. The following results is a generalization of Wiener-Wintner Theorem.

Theorem 7.10
.

[
22
, Theorem 2.22]
[
23
, Theorem 23.14]

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Then there exits a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi(n)f(T^{n}x)

exits for all
x\in X_{0}
and every nilsequence
(\Psi(n))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

Moreover, if
\mathbb{E}(f|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(T))=0
, then the for all
x\in X_{0}
,
\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi(n)f(T^{n}x)=0
.

It is natural to conjecture the following generalization of Theorem
7.10
, which is also a pointwise version of Theorem
7.6
.

Conjecture 2
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and
a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Then there exits a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi(n)f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{a_{m}n}x)

exits for all
x\in X_{0}
and every nilsequence
(\Psi(n))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

When
m=2
, see
[
1
]
for some related results about Conjecture
2
.

Assuming the Conjectures
1
and
2
, we can prove the following pointwise version of Theorem
7.8
:

Theorem 7.11
.

Let
T,S
be ergodic measure preserving transformations acting on a probability space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
such that
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
has zero entropy. Let
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
be integral polynomials with
\deg{p_{i}}\geq 2,1\leq i\leq d
. If Conjectures
1
and
2
hold, then for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m},g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the averages

(7.10)

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{c_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{c_{m}n}x)\cdot S^{p_{1}(n)}g_{1}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n)}g_{d}

converge almost surely.

7.3.2.
Proof of Theorem
7.11

To show the main result of this subsection we need the following

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
T,S
be ergodic measure preserving transformations acting on a probability space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
and
T
has zero entropy. Let
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}
be distinct non-zero integers and let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
be integral polynomials with
\deg{p_{i}}\geq 2,1\leq i\leq d
and satisfying
(\spadesuit)
. If Conjecture
1
holds, then for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
f_{i},g_{j}^{(t)}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),1\leq i\leq m,1\leq t\leq d,-l\leq j\leq l
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
t\in[1,d],j\in[-l,l]
, one has

(7.11)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\big)=0

almost surely.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\infty}:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)\rightarrow(Z_{\infty}(S),\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S),\mu_{\infty},S)

be the factor map, where
Z_{\infty}(S)
is the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)
.
By Theorem
2.1
, we may assume that there exist a probability pace
(U,{\mathcal{U}},\rho)
and a measurable cocycle
\alpha:Z_{\infty}(S)\rightarrow{\rm Aut}(U,\rho)
such that

(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,S)=(Z_{\infty}(S)\times U,\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)\times{\mathcal{U}},\mu_{\infty}\times\rho,S_{\alpha}).

We assume that
X,\ Z_{\infty}(S)
and
U
are all compact metric spaces.

By Proposition
2.23
, one has that if (
7.11
) holds for all continuous functions, then (
7.11
) holds for all bounded measurable functions. Thus it suffices to show (
7.11
) holds for all continuous functions.

By Conjecture
1
and Proposition
7.1
, there is a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that for all
x\in X_{0}
, we have that in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{m})
and
M((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\delta_{\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m}}=\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x}\quad\text{and }\quad\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}={\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},

where
\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}=\big(S^{\vec{p}(n)}x^{\otimes d}\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
S^{\vec{p}(n)}=S^{p_{1}(n)}\times\ldots\times S^{p_{d}(n)}
.

By Theorem
6.4
,

{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}=({\mu_{\infty}})^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}\times(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},

and
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem
((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)},\sigma)
and a Bernoulli system
((U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},({\mathcal{U}}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\sigma)
.

By Lemma
7.4
, there is an
X^{*}\in\mathcal{X}
with a full measure
\mu(X^{*})=1
such that for all
x\in X^{*}
,
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy, where
\vec{c}=(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{m})
.

By the proof of the last equality of (
6.14
) in Theorem
6.1
, there is some
X^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X^{\prime})=1
such that for each
x\in X^{\prime}
and for
({\mu_{\infty}})_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)}^{(\infty)}
-a.e.
{\bf z}\in(Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
,

\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\int_{U}g_{j}^{(1)}d\rho\cdot\cdots\cdot\int_{U}g_{j}^{(d)}d\rho\big)=\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(1)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\cdot\cdots\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(d)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))\big).

Let
x\in X_{0}\cap X^{*}\cap X^{\prime}
and
\lambda_{x}
be a limit point of the following sequence in
{\mathcal{M}}(X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})

\Big\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\delta_{(\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m},\sigma^{n}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})}\Big\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}.

Thus, there is some subsequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathbb{N}
such that

(7.12)

\lambda_{x}=\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\delta_{(\tau_{\vec{c}}^{n}x^{\otimes m},\sigma^{n}\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})},\quad\text{weak${}^{*}$ in ${\mathcal{M}}(X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})$}.

Thus
\lambda_{x}
is a joining of
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{a},x},\tau_{\vec{a}})
and
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
.

Since
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem

((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},\pi_{\infty}(x)},\sigma)

and a Bernoulli system
((U^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},({\mathcal{U}}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\rho^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},\sigma)
, by Lemma
7.2
the
\infty
-step pro-nilfactor
((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is also
the Pinsker factor of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
.

Since
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}^{(t)}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
t\in[1,d],j\in[-l,l]
, by (
7.2.3
)

(7.13)

\mathbb{E}_{{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)|(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}\Big)=0.

Since
(X^{m},\mathcal{X}^{m},\mu^{(m)}_{\vec{c},x},\tau_{\vec{c}})
has zero entropy and
((Z_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{(\mu_{\infty})}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the Pinsker factor of
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
, by Theorem
7.3
and (
7.13
),

(7.14)

\int_{X^{m}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)d\lambda_{x}=0.

By (
7.12
) and (
7.14
), for all
x\in X_{0}\cap X^{*}\cap X^{\prime}
,

\begin{split}&\quad\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\big)\\
&=\int_{X^{s}\times(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\otimes\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}\big(g_{j}^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{j}^{(d)}\big)d\lambda_{x}=0.\end{split}

Since
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is an arbitrary subsequence of
\mathbb{N}
, one has

(7.15)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot\prod_{j=-l}^{l}\big(S^{p_{1}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(1)}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n+j)}g_{j}^{(d)}\big)=0

almost surely.
The proof is complete.
∎

Similar to the proof of Proposition
7.1
, we can remove the condition
(\spadesuit)
in Proposition
7.12
.

Corollary 7.13
.

Let
T,S
be ergodic measure preserving transformations acting on a probability space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
such that
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
has zero entropy. Let
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}
be distinct non-zero integers and let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{d}
be integral polynomials with
\deg{p_{i}}\geq 2,1\leq i\leq d
. If Conjecture
1
holds, then for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
f_{i},g_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu),1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq d
with
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}(g_{j}|\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S))=0
for some
j\in\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
, one has

(7.16)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{c_{1}n}f_{1}\cdots T^{c_{m}n}f_{m}\cdot S^{p_{1}(n)}g_{1}\cdots S^{p_{d}(n)}g_{d}=0

almost surely.

Finally, we can prove Theorem
7.11
.

Proof.

By Corollary
7.13
, in order to prove pointwise convergence
of the averages (
7.10
), we can assume that all functions
g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}
are measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)
.

By Proposition
2.23
, for
f_{1},\ldots,f_{m}
fixed, the family of functions
g_{i}
such that the convergence (
7.10
) holds almost
everywhere is closed in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
. Therefore, in order to prove the existence almost everywhere
of the limit (
7.10
) for
g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}
measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{\infty}(S)
, it suffices to restrict to
the case where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}
are measurable with respect to
\mathcal{Z}_{k}(S)
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
. As showed in the proof of Theorem
7.8
, there is a full measure subset
X_{1}
of
X
for which

(\Psi_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{d}{g}_{j}(S^{p_{j}(n)}x)\Big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a nilsequence.

By Theorem
7.10
there exits a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi(n)f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{a_{m}n}x)

exits for all
x\in X_{0}
and every nilsequence
(\Phi(n))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Now let
X_{2}=X_{0}\cap X_{1}
. Then
\mu(X_{2})=1
, and for all
x\in X_{2}
, the limit

(7.17)

\begin{split}&\quad\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Psi_{n}(x)f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{a_{m}n}x)\\
&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{a_{1}n}x)\cdots f_{m}(T^{a_{m}n}x)\cdot g_{1}(S^{p_{1}(n)}x)\cdots g_{d}(S^{p_{d}(n)}x)\end{split}

exists.
The proof is complete.
∎

8.
Relations with Furstenberg systems

In this section, we study the relations of
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
with Furstenberg systems of sequences, and give a positive answer to Problem 1 in
[
12
]
.

8.1.
Furstenberg systems of sequences

First we introduce the notion of Furstenberg systems of sequences from
[
13
,
14
,
12
]
.

8.1.1.

Let
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
\mathbb{N}
with
N_{k}\to\infty
and
I
be a
compact interval in
\mathbb{R}
. We say that the sequence
z=(z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=(z(n))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
with values in
I

admits correlations
on
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, if the limits

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{k}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}z(n+n_{j})

exist for all
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all
n_{1},\ldots,n_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}
(not necessarily distinct).
Note that for a given sequence
z=(z_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
with values in
I
(i.e.
z:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
), every sequence of
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
has a subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such
that the sequence
z
admits correlations on
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

8.1.2.
Furstenberg system associated with
z

Let
I
be a
compact interval in
\mathbb{R}
.
If a sequence
z:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
admits correlations on a given sequence of positive imtegers, then by the correspondence principle of Furstenberg
[
16
,
17
]
we may associate
a measure preserving system that captures the statistical properties of this sequence.

Let
\Omega=I^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\sigma:\Omega\rightarrow\Omega
be the shift:
(\sigma\omega)(n)=\omega(n+1),\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}
. If a sequence
z:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
admits correlations on
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, then for all
f\in C(\Omega)
the following limit exist

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{k}}f(\sigma^{n}z).

Hence the following limit exists in
{\mathcal{M}}(\Omega)
:

\nu=\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{k}}\delta_{\sigma^{n}z}

and we say that the point
z
is
generic for
\nu
along
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
I
be a compact interval of
\mathbb{R}
and let
z:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
be a sequence that
admits correlations on
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, and
(\Omega,\nu,\sigma)
as above.

•

(\Omega,\nu,\sigma)
is called the
Furstenberg system associated with
z
on
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
.

•

Let
F_{0}\in C(\Omega)
be defined by
F_{0}(\omega)=\omega(0),\forall\omega\in\Omega
, i.e. the
0^{\rm th}
-coordinate projection. Then
F_{0}(\sigma^{n}z)=z(n),\forall n\in\mathbb{Z}
and

(8.1)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N_{k}}\prod_{j=1}^{s}z(n+n_{j})=\int_{\Omega}\prod_{j=1}^{s}\sigma^{n_{j}}F_{0}d\nu

for all
s\in\mathbb{N}
and all
n_{1},\ldots,n_{s}\in\mathbb{Z}
. This identity is referred to as the
Furstenberg
correspondence principle
.

Remark 8.2
.

We have

(1)

A sequence
z:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
may have several non-isomorphic Furstenberg systems
depending on which sequence of
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
we use in the evaluation of its correlations. We call any such system a Furstenberg system of
z
.

(2)

We say that the sequence
z
has a
unique Furstenberg system
, if
z
admits correlations
on
\{N\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, or equivalently,

\nu=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\delta_{\sigma^{n}z}

exists.

8.1.3.
Problems about Furstenberg systems

The following is the Problem 1 in
[
12
]
(see also Remark 3.2 in
[
14
]
).

Problem 1
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., and let
p
be a non-linear integral polynomial, and
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Show that for almost every
x\in X
the sequence
\big(f(T^{p(n)})x\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
has a unique Furstenberg system that is ergodic and isomorphic to a direct product of an infinite-step pro-nilsystem
and a Bernoulli system.

As mentioned in
[
12
]
, proving uniqueness of the Furstenberg
system seems very hard as this amounts to proving a pointwise convergence result for multiple ergodic averages that currently seems out of reach. So as a first step for
an unconditional result, one probably has to compromise with the following problem.

Problem 2
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., and let
p
be a non-linear integral polynomial, and
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Show that for any strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
there is a subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for almost every
x\in X
the the Furstenberg systems of
\big(f(T^{p(n)})x\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
along
\{N_{k}^{\prime}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are ergodic and isomorphic to direct products of infinite-step pro-nilsystems and Bernoulli systems.

8.2.
A corollary of Thouvenot’s and Host-Kra’s theorem

To answer Problems above, we need to use the following theorems to prove that a factor of a direct product of an ergodic pro-nilsystem with a Bernoulli system is still of the same type.

Ornstein showed that Bernoulli systems are closed under taking factors
[
34
]
. Thouvenot extended this result as follows

Theorem 8.3

(Thouvenot)
.

[
40
]

The factor of a direct product of an ergodic entropy zero transformation with a Bernoulli system is still a direct product of ergodic entropy zero transformation with a Bernoulli system.

Another well known fact is the following

Theorem 8.4

(Host-Kra)
.

[
20
, Proposition 4.11]
A factor of
d
-step pro-nilsystem (
d\in\mathbb{N}
) is a
d
-step pro-nilsystem.

Using Thouvenot’s and Host-Kra’s result we can prove the following result:

Theorem 8.5
.

The factor of a direct product of an ergodic pro-nilsystem with a Bernoulli system is still a direct product of an ergodic pro-nilsystem with a Bernoulli system.

Proof.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic pro-nilsystem and let
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
be a Bernoulli system. Let
\pi:(X\times Y,\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y},\mu\times\nu,T\times S)\rightarrow(Z,\mathcal{Z},\varrho,H)
be a factor map. Note that
h_{\mu}(X,T)=0
. By Theorem
8.3
, there is an ergodic m.p.s.
(X^{\prime},X^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},T^{\prime})
with
h_{\mu^{\prime}}(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})=0
and a Bernoulli system
(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\nu^{\prime},S^{\prime})
such that

(Z,\mathcal{Z},\varrho,H)=(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}\times\nu^{\prime},T^{\prime}\times S^{\prime}).

It is well known that the Pinsker factor of the product of two ergodic m.p.s. is the product of their Pinsker factors (see for example
[
19
, Theorem 18.13.]
). Thus

\Pi(X\times Y,T\times S)=\Pi(X,T)\times\Pi(Y,S),\quad\Pi(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime},T^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})=\Pi(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})\times\Pi(Y^{\prime},S^{\prime}).

Since
\Pi(X,T)=\mathcal{X}
,
\Pi(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, and
(Y,\mathcal{Y},\nu,S)
and
(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}^{\prime},\nu^{\prime},S^{\prime})
are Bornoulli systems, it follows
\Pi(X\times Y,T\times S)=\mathcal{X}\times\{Y,\emptyset\}
and
\Pi(X^{\prime}\times Y^{\prime},T^{\prime}\times S^{\prime})=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\{Y^{\prime},\emptyset\}
. Thus
(X^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},T^{\prime})
is a factor of
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
. By Theorem
8.4
,
(X^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},T^{\prime})
is a pro-nilsystem.
∎

8.3.
A lemma about topological models

One can find the following result in
[
16
, Page 222]
. Since in
[
16
]
no detailed proof was given, for completeness we give a proof in the appendix
C
(see Theorem
C.1
).

Theorem 8.6
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. and let
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Then there is a t.d.s.
(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})
, a
T^{\prime}
-invariant measure
\mu^{\prime}
and an isomorphism
\psi:(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)\rightarrow(X^{\prime},\mathcal{B}(X^{\prime}),\mu^{\prime},T^{\prime})

such that
f^{\prime}=f\circ\psi^{-1}\pmod{\mu^{\prime}}
, where
f^{\prime}\in C(X^{\prime})
.

8.4.
Relation between
\infty
-joining and Furstenberg systems of sequences

Now we prove the following result, which gives a positive answer to Problem
2
. Moreover, the remark after the result gives
a positive answer to Problem
1
.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., and let
p
be a non-linear integral polynomial with
p(0)=0
, and
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. Then for any strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
there is a subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for almost every
x\in X
the Furstenberg systems of
\big(f(T^{p(n)})x\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
along
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are ergodic and isomorphic to direct products of infinite-step pro-nilsystems and Bernoulli systems.

Proof.

By Theorem
8.6
, we may assume that
(X,T)
is a t.d.s. and
f
is real-valued and continuous. Let
\mathcal{A}=\{p\}
. By Theorem
6.4
, Theorem
6.6
, Remark
6.5
, and Remark
6.7
, for any strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
\{N_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
there is a subsequence
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and a subset
X_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
with
\mu(X_{0})=1
such that for all
x\in X_{0}
,

\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}={\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\quad w^{*}\ \text{in}\ M(X^{\mathbb{Z}}),

and
(X^{\mathbb{Z}},\mathcal{X}^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is isomorphic to the product of an
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem and a Bernoulli system.

Now let
f:X\rightarrow I
, where
I
is a compact interval of
\mathbb{R}
. Let
z_{x}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow I
be
z_{x}(n)=f(T^{p(n)}x)
. For
x\in X_{0}
, let

\phi:X^{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\Omega=I^{\mathbb{Z}},\quad(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mapsto(f(x_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}.

In particular,
\phi(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}})=\phi\Big((T^{p(n)}x)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Big)=(f(T^{p(n)}x))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=z_{x}
.

Since
f
is continuous, we have that
\phi
is continuous. Then

\phi_{*}:{\mathcal{M}}(X^{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}(\Omega),\theta\mapsto\theta\circ\phi^{-1}

is continuous. Note that

\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}\overset{\phi_{*}}{\mapsto}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}z_{x}}.

Since
\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}\omega^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}={\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}
and
\phi_{*}
is continuous, we have that
\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{\prime}_{k}}\sum_{n=1}^{N^{\prime}_{k}}\delta_{{\sigma}^{n}z_{x}}
exists and denote it by
\nu_{x}
.
By the definition of Furstenberg systems,
(\Omega,\nu_{x},\sigma)
is the Furstenberg system associated with
z
on
\{N^{\prime}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
. And

\phi:(X^{\mathbb{Z}},\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)\rightarrow(\Omega,\nu_{x},\sigma)

is a factor map. By Theorem
8.5
,
(\Omega,\nu_{x},\sigma)
is a direct product of an ergodic pro-nilsystem with a Bernoulli shift. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 8.8
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be an ergodic m.p.s., and let
p
be a non-linear integral polynomial, and
f\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
.
By Remark
6.5
, if one can prove any statement in Proposition
7.1
, then in Theorem
6.4
,
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
is
\{N\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, that is,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\delta_{\widetilde{\sigma}^{n}\xi^{\mathcal{A}}_{x}}=\widetilde{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}.

In this case for almost every
x\in X
the sequence
\big(f(T^{p(n)})x\big)_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
has a unique Furstenberg system. Then by the same analysis above, one can show that it is ergodic and isomorphic to a direct product of an infinite-step pro-nilsystem and a Bernoulli system, which will give the positive answer to Problem
1
.

9.
Questions

In this section, we give some remarks and more questions.

9.1.
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
for general scheme
\mathcal{A}

Let
\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{d}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be a family of functions taking integer values at the
integers, where
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Then in the same sprit of Section
3
, we can define a measure
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
with respect to
\mathcal{A}=\{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{d}\}
.

Let
\vec{\phi}=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\cdots,\phi_{d})
and let
T^{\vec{\phi}(n)}:X^{d}\rightarrow X^{d}

(9.1)

T^{\vec{\phi}(n)}\Big((x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{d})\Big)=(T^{\phi_{1}(n)}x_{1},T^{\phi_{2}(n)}x_{2},\ldots,T^{\phi_{d}(n)}x_{d}).

For each
x\in X
, define

\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}\triangleq(T^{\vec{\phi}(n)}x^{\otimes d})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}=(\ldots,T^{\vec{\phi}(-1)}(x^{\otimes d}),\underset{\bullet}{T^{\vec{\phi}(0)}(x^{\otimes d})},T^{\vec{\phi}(1)}(x^{\otimes d}),T^{\vec{\phi}(2)}(x^{\otimes d}),\cdots)\in(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}.

Let
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
be the measure on
(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that there is some sequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
with

\begin{split}\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}F({\bf x})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})&=\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\int_{X}F(\sigma^{n}(\omega_{x}^{\mathcal{A}}))d\mu(x)\\
&=\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\int_{X}F\big((T^{\vec{\phi}(n+j)}x^{\otimes d})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\big)d\mu(x)\end{split}

for all
F\in C((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}})
, equivalently, for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{j}^{\otimes}=f_{j}^{(1)}\otimes f_{j}^{(2)}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{j}^{(d)}\in C(X^{d}),-l\leq j\leq l
,

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{(X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}\Big)({\bf x})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})\\
&=\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\int_{X}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{\otimes}(T^{\vec{\phi}(n+j)}x^{\otimes d})d\mu(x)\\
&=\lim_{i\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{N_{i}}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{i}-1}\int_{X}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}^{(1)}(T^{\phi_{1}(n+j}x)f_{j}^{(2)}(T^{\phi_{2}(n+j)}x)\cdots f_{j}^{(d)}(T^{\phi_{d}(n+j)}x)d\mu(x).\end{split}

Remark 9.1
.

In general, for
\mathcal{A}=\{\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\ldots,\phi_{d}\}
the measure
\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}
depends on the sequence
\{N_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Question 1
.

For what kind of
\mathcal{A}
, for
\mu
a.e.
x\in X
, the m.p.s.
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x},\sigma)
is the product of a
\infty
-step pro-nilsystem and a Bernoulli system, where
\displaystyle\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}}=\int_{X}\mu^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A},x}d\mu(x)
is the ergodic decomposition of
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}
under
\sigma
.

9.2.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}=\{\mathfrak{p}_{1}<\mathfrak{p}_{2}<\cdots\}
be the set of primes.

Question 2
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be a m.p.s. Then for all non-constant integral polynomials
q_{1},\ldots,q_{d}
and for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, do the averages

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{q_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{n})}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{q_{d}(\mathfrak{p}_{n+d-1})}x)

converge in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
? a.s.?

In particular, do the averages

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T^{\mathfrak{p}_{n}}x)\cdots f_{d}(T^{\mathfrak{p}_{n+d-1}}x)

converge in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
? a.s.?

If Question
2
has a positive answer, then for
\mathcal{A}=\{\phi_{1}(n)=q_{1}(\mathfrak{p}_{n}),\ldots,\phi_{d}(n)=q_{d}(\mathfrak{p}_{n})\}
, one can define the corresponding m.p.s.
((X^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},(\mathcal{X}^{d})^{\mathbb{Z}},{\mu}^{(\infty)}_{\mathcal{A}},\sigma)
.
It is interesting to know its structure and its applications.

Appendix A
From continuous functions to measurable functions

In the paper, we meet the following situation frequently: we first build some convergence results for continuous functions, and then pass them to bounded measurable ones. The proof of this procedure is non-trivial but standard. In this appendix, we give an example to explain the way to do this for completeness. Moreover, we also prove some results mentioned in the previous text.

A.1.
From continuous functions to measurable ones

First we need the following simple but basic fact:

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\{a_{i}\},\{b_{i}\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}
. Then

\prod_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{k}b_{i}=(a_{1}-b_{1})b_{2}\ldots b_{k}+a_{1}(a_{2}-b_{2})b_{3}\ldots b_{k}+\ldots+a_{1}\cdots a_{k-1}(a_{k}-b_{k}).

By this fact we have

Lemma A.2
.

Let
f_{i},f_{i}^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(X_{i},\mathcal{X}_{i},\mu_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,k
and assume that all
|f_{i}|,|f^{\prime}_{i}|
bounded by
M>0
. Then for any standard measure
\lambda
of
\{(X_{i},\mathcal{X}_{i},\mu_{i})\}_{i=1}^{k}
,

\int_{\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}}\left|f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{k}-f^{\prime}_{1}\otimes f_{2}^{\prime}\otimes\cdots\otimes f^{\prime}_{k}\right|d\lambda\leq M^{k-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int_{X_{i}}|f_{i}-f^{\prime}_{i}|d\mu_{i}.

Theorem A.3
.

Let
T_{1},T_{2},\cdots,T_{d}
be invertible measure preserving transformations which act on a Lebesgue space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
(
X
is a compact metric space), and let
a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{d}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be maps. If there is a family
\{\lambda_{x}\}_{x\in X}
of Borel probability measures on
X^{d}
such that

•

\displaystyle\lambda=\int_{X}\lambda_{x}d\mu(x)
is a Borel standard probability measure on
X^{d}
.

•

for all
g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}\in C(X)
, the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}g_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)g_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots g_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)=\int_{X^{d}}g_{1}\otimes g_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{d}d\lambda_{x}

holds in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
,

then for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
,

(1)

for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda_{x})
,

(2)

the limit

(A.1)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)=\int_{X^{d}}f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{d}d\lambda_{x}

holds in
L^{2}(X,\mu)
.

Proof.

Let
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
and let
\epsilon>0
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that for each
1\leq j\leq d
,

\|f_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Choose continuous functions
g_{j}
such that

\|g_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|f_{j}-g_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mu)}\leq\|f_{j}-g_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}<\epsilon
for all
1\leq j\leq d
.

First we show (1). Let
A_{j}=\{x\in X:|f_{j}(x)|\leq\|f_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
. Then
\mu(A_{j})=1
for all
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
. Note that

\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{j}\subseteq\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in X^{d}:\bigotimes_{j=l}^{d}f_{j}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})=\prod_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}(x_{j})\leq 1\}.

Since
\lambda
is a standard measure on
X^{d}
, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}

\lambda(P_{j}^{-1}(A_{j}))=\mu(A_{j})=1
, where
P_{j}:X^{d}\rightarrow X
is the projection to
j
-th coordinate. Thus
\lambda(\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{j})=\lambda(\bigcap_{j=1}^{d}P_{j}^{-1}(A_{j}))=1
. In particular,
\big\|\bigotimes_{j=l}^{d}f_{j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda)}\leq 1

and

\bigotimes_{j=l}^{d}f_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda)
.
And by

\int_{X}\lambda_{x}(\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{j})d\mu(x)=\lambda(\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{j})=1,

we have for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\lambda_{x}(\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{j})=1
. It follows that for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\big\|\bigotimes_{j=l}^{d}f_{j}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda_{x})}\leq 1
and

\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda_{x})
.

Now we show (2). We have

(A.2)

\begin{split}&\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}d\lambda_{x}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
&\leq\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}+\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}d\lambda_{x}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}+\left\|\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}d\lambda_{x}-\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}d\lambda_{x}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}.\end{split}

Since
\mu
is
T_{j}
-invariant, for
1\leq j\leq d
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big|g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\Big|\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\left\|g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{X}\Big(g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\Big)^{2}d\mu(x)\Big)^{1/2}\\
&=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{X}\Big(g_{j}(x)-f_{j}(x)\Big)^{2}d\mu(x)\Big)^{1/2}=\|g_{j}-f_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}.\end{split}

Hence by Lemma
A.1
,

(A.3)

\begin{split}&\quad\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Big(\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\Big|g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)-f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\Big|\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\Big)\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|g_{j}-f_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq d\epsilon.\end{split}

Since
g_{l},g_{2},\ldots g_{d}\in C(X)
, by the assumption of the theorem, we have

(A.4)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}d\lambda_{x}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0.

Let

\widetilde{\lambda}=\lambda\times_{X}\lambda=\int_{X}\lambda_{x}\times\lambda_{x}d\mu(x).

Sine
\lambda
is standard on
X^{d}
, the measure
\widetilde{\lambda}
is standard on
X^{d}\times X^{d}
.
Then

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}d\lambda_{x}-\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}d\lambda_{x}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\\
=&\int_{X}\Big(\int_{X^{d}}\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)d\lambda_{x}\Big)^{2}d\mu(x)\\
=&\int_{X}\Big(\int_{X^{d}\times X^{d}}\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)\otimes\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)d\lambda_{x}\times\lambda_{x}\Big)d\mu(x)\\
=&\int_{X^{d}\times X^{d}}\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)\otimes\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)d\widetilde{\lambda}.\end{split}

Because
\lambda
is a standard measure, by Lemma
A.2
we have

\begin{split}&\int_{X^{d}\times X^{d}}\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)\otimes\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big)d\widetilde{\lambda}\\
\leq&\int_{X^{d}\times X^{d}}\big|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big|\otimes\Big(\big|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}\big|+\big|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big|\Big)d\widetilde{\lambda}\\
\leq&2\int_{X^{d}\times X^{d}}\big|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big|\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}1d\widetilde{\lambda}=2\int_{X^{d}}\big|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}-\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}\big|d{\lambda}\\
\leq&2\sum_{j=1}^{d}\int_{X}|f_{j}-g_{j}|d\mu\leq 2\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|f_{j}-g_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq 2d\epsilon.\end{split}

It follows that

(A.5)

\left\|\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}d\lambda_{x}-\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}g_{j}d\lambda_{x}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq\sqrt{2d\epsilon}.

So combining (
A.2
)-(
A.5
), we have

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\left\|\int_{X^{d}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}d\lambda_{x}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}(T_{j}^{a_{j}(n)}x)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq d\epsilon+\sqrt{2d\epsilon}.

Since
\epsilon
is arbitrary, the proof is complete.
∎

Remark A.4
.

In Theorem
A.3
, if we only want to show that
\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}g_{1}\cdots T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}g_{d}
holding in
L^{2}(\mu)
for continuous functions implies that it holds for bounded measure functions, then the proof will be much simpler.

Definition A.5
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
be m.p.s., and let
a:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be a map. We say that
T

satisfies the condition (B) w.r.t.
a

, if for all
p>1
there exists a constant
C(p,a)>0
such that for all
f\in L^{p}(X,\mu)
,

(B)

\Big\|\sup_{N>0}\big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T^{a(n)}f\big|\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mu)}\leq C(p,a)\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mu)}.

By Theorem
2.21
, any m.p.s.
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu,T)
satisfies the condition (B) w.r.t.
p
, where
p:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is a polynomial.

Theorem A.6
.

Let
T_{1},T_{2},\cdots,T_{d}
be invertible measure preserving transformations which act on a Lebesgue space
(X,\mathcal{X},\mu)
(
X
is a compact metric space), and let
a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{d}:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
be maps. Assume that for each
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,d\}
,
T_{i}
satisfies the condition (B) w.r.t.
a_{i}
.
If there is a family
\{\lambda_{x}\}_{x\in X}
of Borel probability measures on
X^{d}
such that

•

\displaystyle\lambda=\int_{X}\lambda_{x}\mu(x)
is a Borel standard probability measures on
X^{d}
.

•

for all
g_{1},\ldots,g_{d}\in C(X)
, the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}g_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)g_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots g_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)=\int_{X^{d}}g_{1}\otimes g_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes g_{d}d\lambda_{x}

holds for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,

then for all
f_{1},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
, the limit

(A.6)

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)=\int_{X^{d}}f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{d}d\lambda_{x}

exists for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
.

Proof.

Let
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{d}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
. By Theorem
A.3
, for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
,
\bigotimes_{j=1}^{d}f_{j}\in L^{\infty}(X^{d},\lambda_{x})
. First
we show that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)

exists for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
. The proof is standard, we follow the proof of
[
10
, Corollary 2.2]
. After that we show (
A.6
) holds.

Let
(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
be the limit of
\Big\{(f_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,f_{d}^{(k)})\Big\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
in
L^{2d}(X,\mu)\times\cdots\times L^{2d}(X,\mu)
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
f_{1}^{(k)},\ldots,f_{d}^{(k)}\in C(X)
. Thus by the assumption, we have

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f^{(k)}_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f^{(k)}_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f^{(k)}_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)

exists for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
.
For a
d
-tuple
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d})
, let

\begin{split}R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d};x)&=\limsup_{N,M\to\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}g_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)g_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots g_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\frac{1}{M}\sum_{n=0}^{M-1}g_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)g_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots g_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)\Big|.\end{split}

To prove the result, it suffices to establish that

(A.7)

\|R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d};x)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0.

Claim:
There is some constant
C=C(d,a_{1},\ldots,a_{d})>0
such that for all
d
-tuple
(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d})\in L^{2d}(X,\mu)\times\cdots\times L^{2d}(X,\mu)
, one has

\|R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d};\cdot)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq C\prod_{i=1}^{d}\|g_{i}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}.

Proof of Claim:
By the Hölder inequality
5
5

5

Here we use the following Hölder inequality: Let
p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}>1
with
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p_{n}}=1
and let
\{a_{i}^{(j)}\}_{i=1}^{m}\subseteq\mathbb{R},1\leq j\leq n
. Then

\sum_{i=1}^{m}a_{i}^{(1)}a_{i}^{(2)}\cdots a_{i}^{(n)}\leq(\sum_{i=1}^{m}|a_{i}^{(1)}|^{p_{1}})^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}(\sum_{i=1}^{m}|a_{i}^{(2)}|^{p_{2}})^{\frac{1}{p_{2}}}\cdots(\sum_{i=1}^{m}|a_{i}^{(n)}|^{p_{n}})^{\frac{1}{p_{n}}}.

\begin{split}R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d};x)\leq 2\sup_{N>0}\Big|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\prod_{i=1}^{d}g_{i}(T_{i}^{a_{i}(n)}x)\Big|\leq 2\prod_{i=1}^{d}\Big(\sup_{N>0}\big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}|g_{i}|^{d}(T_{i}^{a_{i}(n)}x)\big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{d}}.\end{split}

Again by the Hölder inequality
6
6

6

Here we use the following Hölder inequality: Let
p,p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}\geq 1
with
\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{p_{n}}=\frac{1}{p}
and let
f_{1}\in L^{p_{1}}(X,\mu),\ldots,f_{n}\in L^{p_{n}}(X,\mu)
. Then
f_{1}f_{2}\cdots f_{n}\in L^{p}(X,\mu)
and

\|f_{1}f_{2}\cdots f_{n}\|_{L^{p}(X,\mu)}\leq\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p_{1}}(X,\mu)}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{p_{2}}(X,\mu)}\cdots\|f_{n}\|_{L^{p_{n}}(X,\mu)},
that is,

\Big(\int_{X}|f_{1}f_{2}\cdots f_{n}|^{p}d\mu\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\Big(\int_{X}|f_{1}|^{p_{1}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}\Big(\int_{X}|f_{2}|^{p_{2}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{2}}}\cdots\Big(\int_{X}|f_{n}|^{p_{n}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{n}}}

and the condition (B),

\begin{split}\|R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{d};x)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq&2\Big\|\prod_{i=1}^{d}\Big(\sup_{N>0}\big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T_{i}^{a_{i}(n)}|g_{i}|^{d}\big)\Big)^{\frac{1}{d}}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
\leq&2\prod_{i=1}^{d}\Big\|\sup_{N>0}\big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}T_{i}^{a_{i}(n)}|g_{i}|^{d}\big)\Big\|^{\frac{1}{d}}_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
\leq&2\prod_{i=1}^{d}C(2,a_{i})\Big\||g_{i}|^{d}\Big\|^{\frac{1}{d}}_{L^{2}(\mu)}=2\prod_{i=1}^{d}C(2,a_{i})\big\|g_{i}\big\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}.\end{split}

This ends the proof of Claim.

\square

Note that for
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,

R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{j}+g^{\prime}_{j},\ldots,g_{d};x)\leq R(g_{1},\ldots,g_{j},\ldots,g_{d};x)+R(g_{1},\ldots,g^{\prime}_{j},\ldots,g_{d};x).

Combining this with the assumption
R(f^{(k)}_{1},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d};x)=0
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, one has that for almost all
x\in X
,

\begin{split}&R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1},f_{d};x)\\
\leq&R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1},f_{d}-f^{(k)}_{d};x)+R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1},f^{(k)}_{d};x)\\
\leq&R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1},f_{d}-f^{(k)}_{d};x)+R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1}-f^{(k)}_{d-1},f_{d}^{(k)};x)+R(f_{1},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d-1},f^{(k)}_{d};x)\\
\leq&\ldots\\
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{i-1},f_{i}-f_{i}^{(k)},f_{i+1}^{(k)},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d};x)+R(f^{(k)}_{1},f^{(k)}_{2},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d};x)\\
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{i-1},f_{i}-f_{i}^{(k)},f_{i+1}^{(k)},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d};x).\end{split}

By the Claim, we have

\begin{split}&\|R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{d-1},f_{d};x)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}\|R(f_{1},\ldots,f_{i-1},f_{i}-f_{i}^{(k)},f_{i+1}^{(k)},\ldots,f^{(k)}_{d};x)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\\
\leq&\sum_{i=1}^{d}C\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}\cdots\|f_{i-1}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}\|f_{i}-f_{i}^{(k)}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}\|f_{i+1}^{(k)}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}\cdots\|f^{(k)}_{d}\|_{L^{2d}(\mu)}\\
\rightarrow&0,\quad k\to\infty.\end{split}

Then we have
(\ref {a1})
, and hence

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)

holds for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
.
By Theorem
A.3
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\Big\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)-\int_{X^{d}}f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{d}d\lambda_{x}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0.

Thus the limit

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}f_{1}(T_{1}^{a_{1}(n)}x)f_{2}(T_{2}^{a_{2}(n)}x)\cdots f_{d}(T_{2}^{a_{d}(n)}x)=\int_{X^{d}}f_{1}\otimes f_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{d}d\lambda_{x}

exists for
\mu
-a.e.
x\in X
. The proof is complete.
∎

A.2.
Proof of Proposition
3.4

In Theorem
A.3
and Theorem
A.6
, we deal with the standard measure on the product spaces of finitely many systems. But in the paper, we mostly deal with the standard measure on the product spaces of infinitely many systems. The proofs of related results are similar. We take an example in this subsection.

The proof of Proposition
3.4
is similar but simpler to the proof of Theorem
A.3
.

Proof of Proposition
3.4
.

We only show a special case when
\mathcal{A}=\{p\}
, and the general case is similar. In this case
d=1
. So by the definition, for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and all
f_{-l},\ldots,f_{l}\in C(X)
,

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{X^{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}\Big)({\bf x})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})=\int_{X^{\mathbb{Z}}}\big(f_{-l}\otimes f_{-l+1}\otimes\cdots\otimes f_{l}\big)({\bf x})d\mu_{\mathcal{A}}^{(\infty)}({\bf x})\\
&=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\int_{X}\prod_{j=-l}^{l}f_{j}(T^{p(n+j)}x)d\mu(x),\end{split}

where
{\bf x}=(x_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\in X^{\mathbb{Z}}
. We show that this equality still holds if replace functions in
C(X)
by functions in
L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
.

Fix
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{-l},f_{-l+1},\ldots,f_{l}\in L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
and let
\epsilon>0
. Without loss of generality, we assume that for all
-l\leq j\leq l
,
